“瞧这个，”他答道，递给她一小张传单；“方才在花园座椅底下捡的。”

玛丽莫名所以地望了丈夫一眼，读道：——

亚伯拉罕的新拣选之种

本年内将应验的诸预言

-

一百四十四艘船只组成的船队驶往他施及诸岛。

-

狗的权势之毁灭，包括一切反亚伯拉罕立法之器具。

-

船队自他施返航，载阿拉伯之黄金，此金将为亚伯拉罕新城之根基。

-

寻觅新娘，并将印授予七十又七人。

-

圣父之面容将放光，其荣光更胜摩西之面。

-

罗马教皇将在名叫贝雷克-齐托的山谷中被石头打死。

-

圣父将被三大君主承认。将有二大君主否认圣父，并立即灭于圣父震怒之秽气中。

-

捆绑那有小角的兽，众法官尽被推翻。

-

在埃及地寻得新娘，此地已启示给圣父，即今存在于伦敦西部。

-

新舌赐予七十又七人与一百四十四人。圣父进入新娘之内室。

-

伦敦之毁灭与“挪”城之重建，挪城即亚伯拉罕新城。

-

圣父与新娘合体，现今之大地移于太阳，为期半时。

读到这些在她看来纵然语无伦次却也无害的字句，达内尔太太的眉头舒展了。听丈夫的口气，她还当出了什么更切实的不愉快，比这一串虚渺渺的预言经文要实在的。

“嗯，”她说，“这又如何？”

“如何？你不明白吗？这是你姨妈掉的，可见她是个疯得没了治的疯子。”

===Original===

“Oh, Edward! don’t say that. In the first place, how do you know that aunt dropped it at all? It might easily have blown over from any of the other gardens. And, if it were hers, I don’t think you should call her a lunatic. I don’t believe, myself, that there are any real prophets now; but there are many good people who think quite differently. I knew an old lady once who, I am sure, was very good, and she took in a paper every week that was full of prophecies and things very like this. Nobody called her mad, and I have heard father say that she had one of the sharpest heads for business he had ever come across.”

“Very good; have it as you like. But I believe we shall both be sorry.”

They sat in silence for some time. Alice came in after her “evening out,” and they sat on, till Mrs. Darnell said she was tired and wanted to go to bed.

Her husband kissed her. “I don’t think I will come up just yet,” he said; “you go to sleep, dearest. I want to think things over. No, no; I am not going to change my mind: your aunt shall come, as I said. But there are one or two things I should like to get settled in my mind.”

He meditated for a long while, pacing up and down the room. Light after light was extinguished in Edna Road, and the people of the suburb slept all around him, but still the gas was alight in Darnell’s drawing-room, and he walked softly up and down the floor. He was thinking that about the life of Mary and himself, which had been so quiet, there seemed to be gathering on all sides grotesque and fantastic shapes, omens of confusion and disorder, threats of madness; a strange company from another world. It was as if into the quiet, sleeping streets of some little ancient town among the hills there had come from afar the sound of drum and pipe, snatches of wild song, and there had burst into the marketplace the mad company of the players, strangely bedizened, dancing a furious measure to their hurrying music, drawing forth the citizens from their sheltered homes and peaceful lives, and alluring them to mingle in the significant figures of their dance.

===Chinese===

“哦，爱德华！别这么说。第一，你怎么知道一定是姨妈掉的？风从哪家园子里刮过来也说不定。再说，就算是她的，我也不觉得该管她叫疯子。我自己是不信如今还有真先知；可有许多好人想法同我完全两样。我从前认得一位老太太，我敢保她心肠顶好，她每星期订一份报纸，满纸的预言，同这个很相像。没人说她疯；我还听父亲说过，论做生意的头脑，她是他见过顶尖的几位之一。”

“很好；随你怎么说。可我信，咱们俩日后都要后悔。”

他们闷坐了半晌。爱丽丝“歇工外出”回来了；两人还坐着，直坐到达内尔太太说乏了、想上床了。

丈夫吻了她。“我这就暂不上去，”他说，“你先睡，最亲爱的。我想把事情理一理。不，不；我不改主意：你姨妈来，照我说的办。只是有一两件事，我想在心里定下来。”

他来回踱着，思量了许久。埃德娜路的灯一盏一盏灭了，四郊的人都在他身边睡着，只有达内尔家客厅的煤气灯还亮着；他在地板上轻轻走来走去。他想的是：他和玛丽的这份宁静生活四下里，仿佛正围拢来一群怪诞奇诡的形相，是混乱无序的凶兆，疯狂的威胁；一支来自另一个世界的奇异的队伍。仿佛一座群山之间古老小镇静睡的街巷，忽然从远处传来鼓声和笛声、野歌的断片，一群疯癫的戏子闯进了集市广场，妆扮得光怪陆离，就着急促的音乐狂舞，把市民们从安居乐业里拽出来，诱他们加入他们舞蹈里那意味深长的图形。

===Original===

Yet afar and near (for it was hidden in his heart) he beheld the glimmer of a sure and constant star. Beneath, darkness came on, and mists and shadows closed about the town. The red, flickering flame of torches was kindled in the midst of it. The song grew louder, with more insistent, magical tones, surging and falling in unearthly modulations, the very speech of incantation; and the drum beat madly, and the pipe shrilled to a scream, summoning all to issue forth, to leave their peaceful hearths; for a strange rite was preconized in their midst. The streets that were wont to be so still, so hushed with the cool and tranquil veils of darkness, asleep beneath the patronage of the evening star, now danced with glimmering lanterns, resounded with the cries of those who hurried forth, drawn as by a magistral spell; and the songs swelled and triumphed, the reverberant beating of the drum grew louder, and in the midst of the awakened town the players, fantastically arrayed, performed their interlude under the red blaze of torches. He knew not whether they were players, men that would vanish suddenly as they came, disappearing by the track that climbed the hill; or whether they were indeed magicians, workers of great and efficacious spells, who knew the secret word by which the earth may be transformed into the hall of Gehenna, so that they that gazed and listened, as at a passing spectacle, should be entrapped by the sound and the sight presented to them, should be drawn into the elaborated figures of that mystic dance, and so should be whirled away into those unending mazes on the wild hills that were abhorred, there to wander forevermore.

But Darnell was not afraid, because of the Daystar that had risen in his heart. It had dwelt there all his life, and had slowly shone forth with clearer and clearer light, and he began to see that though his earthly steps might be in the ways of the ancient town that was beset by the Enchanters, and resounded with their songs and their processions, yet he dwelt also in that serene and secure world of brightness, and from a great and unutterable height looked on the confusion of the mortal pageant, beholding mysteries in which he was no true actor, hearing magic songs that could by no means draw him down from the battlements of the high and holy city.

===Chinese===

然而，远也罢近也罢（因为它藏在他心里），他望得见一颗安稳恒久之星的微光。底下，黑暗降临，雾和影围拢了小镇。镇子当中，火把的红焰点燃了。歌声更高了，调子更具咒力，汹涌起伏，全是非人间的旋法，简直是念咒的言语；鼓疯狂地敲，笛尖啸到变声，召唤众人出来，离开安乐的炉灶；因为一场奇异的仪典正在他们当中宣告。惯于在夜的清凉安静帷幕下、在长庚星的护佑里酣睡的那些街巷，如今满是摇曳的灯笼在舞动，回响着被咒语牵引着涌出的人们的呼喊；歌声涨起来、得胜了，鼓的回响更响；在醒来的小镇当中，装扮奇诡的戏子们在火把的红光里演着他们的插戏。他不知道他们是戏子，是说来就来、说没就没、顺着上山的路遁走的人呢；还是当真是一班法师，会作大而灵验的法术，知道那句秘字，凭它可以叫大地变成地狱的大厅，叫那些当作过眼热闹看和听的人，被那声音和景象设下的圈套困住，被引进那神秘舞蹈的繁复图形里，被卷进那可憎的荒山上没有尽头的迷宫，从此永远彷徨。

可达内尔不怕，因为那颗晨星已在他心里升起。它一辈子都住在那里，光芒渐渐越放越清亮；他开始明白：纵然他尘世的脚步或许走在那座被术士们围困、回响着他们的歌与行列的古老公道上，他同时也住在那个明净安稳的光明世界里，从一个莫可名状的高处，俯瞰尘世队列的纷乱，旁观他并非真正演员的奥秘，聆听断然不能把他从高圣之城的城堞上拽下去的魔歌。

===Original===

His heart was filled with a great joy and a great peace as he lay down beside his wife and fell asleep, and in the morning, when he woke up, he was glad.

===Chinese===

他心里盛着大喜与大安，躺到妻子身旁睡着了；早晨醒来，他是欢喜的。

### IV / IV

### IV / 
===Original===

In a haze as of a dream Darnell’s thoughts seemed to move through the opening days of the next week. Perhaps nature had not intended that he should be practical or much given to that which is usually called “sound common sense,” but his training had made him desirous of good, plain qualities of the mind, and he uneasily strove to account to himself for his strange mood of the Sunday night, as he had often endeavoured to interpret the fancies of his boyhood and early manhood. At first he was annoyed by his want of success; the morning paper, which he always secured as the bus delayed at Uxbridge Road Station, fell from his hands unread, while he vainly reasoned, assuring himself that the threatened incursion of a whimsical old woman, though tiresome enough, was no rational excuse for those curious hours of meditation in which his thoughts seemed to have dressed themselves in unfamiliar, fantastic habits, and to parley with him in a strange speech, and yet a speech that he had understood.

With such arguments he perplexed his mind on the long, accustomed ride up the steep ascent of Holland Park, past the incongruous hustle of Notting Hill Gate, where in one direction a road shows the way to the snug, somewhat faded bowers and retreats of Bayswater, and in another one sees the portal of the murky region of the slums. The customary companions of his morning’s journey were in the seats about him; he heard the hum of their talk, as they disputed concerning politics, and the man next to him, who came from Acton, asked him what he thought of the Government now. There was a discussion, and a loud and excited one, just in front, as to whether rhubarb was a fruit or vegetable, and in his ear he heard Redman, who was a near neighbour, praising the economy of “the wife.”

“I don’t know how she does it. Look here; what do you think we had yesterday? Breakfast: fish-cakes, beautifully fried﻿—rich, you know, lots of herbs, it’s a receipt of her aunt’s; you should just taste ’em. Coffee, bread, butter, marmalade, and, of course, all the usual etceteras. Dinner: roast beef, Yorkshire, potatoes, greens, and horseradish sauce, plum tart, cheese. And where will you get a better dinner than that? Well, I call it wonderful, I really do.”

===Chinese===

下一周开头的几天，达内尔的念头像是在一层梦的薄雾里穿行。天性也许本就没打算让他做个务实的人，或多给他那种通常唤作“健全常识”的东西；可他受的教养使他向往良善平实的心地，于是星期天夜里那阵古怪的心绪，叫他不安地思量着要给自己个说法，正如他从前也常常这样去解少年和青年时代的种种奇想。起初他恼恨自己不得要领；每天早晨公共汽车在厄克斯布里奇路站停靠时他照例买下的晨报，从手里落下去也没读；他徒然地讲道理，宽慰自己说：一个心血来潮的老太婆要打进来，虽然够烦人，却怎么也够不上他那些奇异的沉思时刻的正当理由——在那几个小时里，他的思想仿佛换上了陌生的奇装，用一种古怪的语言同他交谈；可那语言，他听得懂。

一路乘着车，他就拿这类道理折磨自己：车先爬上荷兰园的陡坡，经过诺丁山门那不相称的熙攘——朝一个方向，一条路通向贝斯沃特那些安逸而略显褪色的闺阁幽居；朝另一个方向，望得见贫民窟那阴暗地界的门户。晨间同路的老伙伴们坐在他四周；他听得见他们谈政治的嗡嗡声；身边一个阿克顿来的男人问他，如今这届政府他怎么看。前头刚起了一场争论，嗓门又高又激，争大黄到底是水果还是蔬菜；耳边又听见近邻雷德曼夸“内子”的节俭。

“我真不懂她怎么弄的。你听我说；你猜咱们昨天吃的什么？早饭：炸鱼饼，炸得漂亮——油润润的，知道吧，香料搁得足足的，她姨母的方子；你真该尝尝。咖啡、面包、黄油、橘子酱，当然，还有老一套的零碎。正餐：烤牛肉、约克郡布丁、土豆、青菜、辣根汁、李子塔、奶酪。你说，上哪儿再找一顿比这强的正餐去？嗐，我叫它了不得，真叫它了不得。”

===Original===

But in spite of these distractions he fell into a dream as the bus rolled and tossed on its way Citywards, and still he strove to solve the enigma of his vigil of the night before, and as the shapes of trees and green lawns and houses passed before his eyes, and as he saw the procession moving on the pavement, and while the murmur of the streets sounded in his ears, all was to him strange and unaccustomed, as if he moved through the avenues of some city in a foreign land. It was, perhaps, on these mornings, as he rode to his mechanical work, that vague and floating fancies that must have long haunted his brain began to shape themselves, and to put on the form of definite conclusions, from which he could no longer escape, even if he had wished it. Darnell had received what is called a sound commercial education, and would therefore have found very great difficulty in putting into articulate speech any thought that was worth thinking; but he grew certain on these mornings that the “common sense” which he had always heard exalted as man’s supremest faculty was, in all probability, the smallest and least-considered item in the equipment of an ant of average intelligence. And with this, as an almost necessary corollary, came a firm belief that the whole fabric of life in which he moved was sunken, past all thinking, in the grossest absurdity; that he and all his friends and acquaintances and fellow-workers were interested in matters in which men were never meant to be interested, were pursuing aims which they were never meant to pursue, were, indeed, much like fair stones of an altar serving as a pigsty wall. Life, it seemed to him, was a great search for﻿—he knew not what; and in the process of the ages one by one the true marks upon the ways had been shattered, or buried, or the meaning of the words had been slowly forgotten; one by one the signs had been turned awry, the true entrances had been thickly overgrown, the very way itself had been diverted from the heights to the depths, till at last the race of pilgrims had become hereditary stone-breakers and ditch-scourers on a track that led to destruction﻿—if it led anywhere at all. Darnell’s heart thrilled with a strange and trembling joy, with a sense that was all new, when it came to his mind that this great loss might not be a hopeless one, that perhaps the difficulties were by no means insuperable.

===Chinese===

可纵有这些分心的事，公共汽车朝城里一路颠簸摇晃，他还是坠入了梦；他仍在苦解昨夜守夜的那个哑谜；树影、绿茵和房屋的轮廓在他眼前过去，人行道上的行列在移动，街市的低响在他耳中，一切于他都陌生、不惯，仿佛他走在异邦某座城的通衢上。也许正是在这些早晨，在他去干那份机械活计的路上，那些必定久已出没他脑际的飘忽幻想开始成形，化作一条条再也躲不开的定论——纵使他想躲。达内尔受的是所谓健全的职业教育，因此要把任何值得想一想的思想说成明白话语，对他是千难万难；可这些早晨他越来越确信：他素来听人捧为人类至高官能的“常识”，多半不过是中等智力蚂蚁的全副装备里最小、最不起眼的一项。随之而来的，几乎是必然的推论，是一个坚定的信念：他所活动的整座人生架构，荒唐透顶，没得救了；他和他所有的朋友、相识、同事，热心的是人本不该热心的事，追求的是人本不该追求的目标；说实在的，很像祭坛的美石被挪去砌了猪圈墙。在他看来，人生是一场对——他不知对什么的伟大寻找；在世代的过程里，路上一枚枚真正的路标被砸碎了、埋没了，或那些字句的含意慢慢被人忘了；一块块指路牌被拧歪了，真正的入口封满了荒草，道路本身也从高处被引到了低处；到末了，朝圣者的族类世世代代成了砸石头、淘阴沟的苦力，守着一条通向毁灭的路——倘若它通向任何地方的话。达内尔的心因一种奇异而战栗的欢喜而颤动，因一种全然崭新的感觉：这重大的损失，也许并非无可挽回；那些困难，也许远非不可克服。

===Original===

It might be, he considered, that the stone-breaker had merely to throw down his hammer and set out, and the way would be plain before him; and a single step would free the delver in rubbish from the foul slime of the ditch.

It was, of course, with difficulty and slowly that these things became clear to him. He was an English City clerk, “flourishing” towards the end of the nineteenth century, and the rubbish heap that had been accumulating for some centuries could not be cleared away in an instant. Again and again the spirit of nonsense that had been implanted in him as in his fellows assured him that the true world was the visible and tangible world, the world in which good and faithful letter-copying was exchangeable for a certain quantum of bread, beef, and houseroom, and that the man who copied letters well, did not beat his wife, nor lose money foolishly, was a good man, fulfilling the end for which he had been made. But in spite of these arguments, in spite of their acceptance by all who were about him, he had the grace to perceive the utter falsity and absurdity of the whole position. He was fortunate in his entire ignorance of sixpenny “science,” but if the whole library had been projected into his brain it would not have moved him to “deny in the darkness that which he had known in the light.” Darnell knew by experience that man is made a mystery for mysteries and visions, for the realization in his consciousness of ineffable bliss, for a great joy that transmutes the whole world, for a joy that surpasses all joys and overcomes all sorrows. He knew this certainly, though he knew it dimly; and he was apart from other men, preparing himself for a great experiment.

===Chinese===

也许，他想，砸石头的只需撂下锤子动身，路就在他眼前铺展；踏进沟里刨垃圾的，一步就能挣脱阴沟的污淖。

当然，这些事是对他慢慢地、艰难地才清楚起来的。他是十九世纪末“蒸蒸日上”的英国城里职员；堆了几个世纪的垃圾堆，不能一眨眼清掉。那种植入他身上、也植入他同类身上的胡闹精神，一而再、再而三地向他保证：真实的世界就是看得见摸得着的世界；在那个世界里，忠实抄写的信件可以换来定量的面包、牛肉和住房；抄信抄得好、不打老婆、不胡乱亏钱的人，就是好人，成就了他被造出来的目的。可任凭这些道理，任凭四下里人人奉它为准，他蒙了恩，看破了整套立场的全然虚假与荒唐。他幸而对六便士一本的“科学”一窍不通；可哪怕整座图书馆灌进他脑子，也不能叫他在黑暗里否认他在光里认过的东西。达内尔凭经验知道：人为奥秘和异象而造，为的是在他意识里实现那不可言说的至福，为的是那点化全世界的欢喜，为的是那胜过一切欢喜、克服一切忧伤的欢喜。这一点他确凿地知道，虽然知道得朦胧；他与众不同，正在给自己预备一场大实验。

===Original===

With such thoughts as these for his secret and concealed treasure, he was able to bear the threatened invasion of Mrs. Nixon with something approaching indifference. He knew, indeed, that her presence between his wife and himself would be unwelcome to him, and he was not without grave doubts as to the woman’s sanity; but after all, what did it matter? Besides, already a faint glimmering light had risen within him that showed the profit of self-negation, and in this matter he had preferred his wife’s will to his own. *Et non sua poma*; to his astonishment he found a delight in denying himself his own wish, a process that he had always regarded as thoroughly detestable. This was a state of things which he could not in the least understand; but, again, though a member of a most hopeless class, living in the most hopeless surroundings that the world has ever seen, though he knew as much of the askesis as of Chinese metaphysics; again, he had the grace not to deny the light that had begun to glimmer in his soul.

===Chinese===

揣着这些念头，当作他秘密隐藏的珍宝，尼克松太太那场迫在眉睫的入侵，他也就能以近乎淡漠的心情对付了。他当然明白，她夹在他和妻子中间是他不愿的；对那女人的神智，他也不是没有重重的疑虑；可说到底，那又算得了什么？再说，他心里已经升起一线微光，照见克己的益处；在这件事上，他已经把妻子的意愿放在了自己的意愿之前。Et non sua poma（不食己果）；叫他吃惊的是，他发现否决自己的心愿竟能得着乐趣——这过程他素来视为顶顶讨厌的。这是一种他全然不能理解的境况；可是，话又说回来，他虽属于一个顶无望的阶级，住在世人见过的顶无望的环境里，对灵修之学的了解同中国玄学一样少；他到底蒙了恩，不去否认那道已在他灵魂里闪烁起来的光。

===Original===

And he found a present reward in the eyes of Mary, when she welcomed him home after his foolish labours in the cool of the evening. They sat together, hand in hand, under the mulberry tree, at the coming of the dusk, and as the ugly walls about them became obscure and vanished into the formless world of shadows, they seemed to be freed from the bondage of Shepherd’s Bush, freed to wander in that undisfigured, undefiled world that lies beyond the walls. Of this region Mary knew little or nothing by experience, since her relations had always been of one mind with the modern world, which has for the true country an instinctive and most significant horror and dread. Mr. Reynolds had also shared in another odd superstition of these later days﻿—that it is necessary to leave London at least once a year; consequently Mary had some knowledge of various seaside resorts on the south and east coasts, where Londoners gather in hordes, turn the sands into one vast, bad music-hall, and derive, as they say, enormous benefit from the change. But experiences such as these give but little knowledge of the country in its true and occult sense; and yet Mary, as she sat in the dusk beneath the whispering tree, knew something of the secret of the wood, of the valley shut in by high hills, where the sound of pouring water always echoes from the clear brook. And to Darnell these were nights of great dreams; for it was the hour of the work, the time of transmutation, and he who could not understand the miracle, who could scarcely believe in it, yet knew, secretly and half consciously, that the water was being changed into the wine of a new life. This was ever the inner music of his dreams, and to it he added on these still and sacred nights the far-off memory of that time long ago when, a child, before the world had overwhelmed him, he journeyed down to the old grey house in the west, and for a whole month heard the murmur of the forest through his bedroom window, and when the wind was hushed, the washing of the tides about the reeds; and sometimes awaking very early he had heard the strange cry of a bird as it rose from its nest among the reeds, and had looked out and had seen the valley whiten to the dawn, and the winding river whiten as it swam down to the sea.

===Chinese===

而眼见玛丽在傍晚凉快时迎他收工回家的目光，他得到了现下的报偿。黄昏时分，两人手拉手坐在桑树下；四周丑陋的墙渐渐模糊、消融进影子的无形世界，他们仿佛挣脱了谢泼德布什的捆绑，得了自由，得以漫游在那高墙之外未遭玷污的洁净世界。那个地方，玛丽凭经验几乎一无所知，因为她的亲人们向来同现代世界一个鼻孔出气——现代世界对真正的乡野，怀着一种出于天性的、意味深长的恐惧与憎厌。雷诺兹先生还沾染了晚近另一桩古怪的迷信——每年至少离开伦敦一次；因此玛丽认得南部和东部海岸的几处海边胜地，伦敦人成群结队涌到那里，把沙滩变成一座庞大的蹩脚音乐厅，并且，照他们自己的说法，从换空气里获得无穷的好处。可这样的经历，对真正意义的、隐秘意义的乡野，所知甚少；然而玛丽坐在低语的大树下的暮色里，也懂得几分树林的秘密，懂得那高山围拢的谷地的秘密——那里，倾泻的水声永远从清澈的小溪回响。对达内尔，这些夜晚是大梦的夜晚；因为那是功行的时辰，点化的时辰；他不懂这奇迹，几乎不敢信它，却秘密地、半意识地知道：水正在变成新生命的酒。这是他梦境深处恒久的音乐；在这些静穆神圣的夜晚，他还给它添上远远的旧忆——很久以前，他还是个孩子，世界还没有淹没他，他到西边那座灰色老宅去，整月整月听森林的低语从卧室窗口涌进来，风静的时候，听潮水在芦苇四周的洗荡；有时醒得极早，他听见一只鸟从芦苇丛里的巢上飞起时那声怪叫，望出去，望见山谷向黎明泛白，蜿蜒的河在游向大海的一路泛白。

===Original===

The memory of all this had faded and become shadowy as he grew older and the chains of common life were riveted firmly about his soul; all the atmosphere by which he was surrounded was well-nigh fatal to such thoughts, and only now and again in half-conscious moments or in sleep he had revisited that valley in the far-off west, where the breath of the wind was an incantation, and every leaf and stream and hill spoke of great and ineffable mysteries. But now the broken vision was in great part restored to him, and looking with love in his wife’s eyes he saw the gleam of water-pools in the still forest, saw the mists rising in the evening, and heard the music of the winding river.

They were sitting thus together on the Friday evening of the week that had begun with that odd and half-forgotten visit of Mrs. Nixon, when, to Darnell’s annoyance, the doorbell gave a discordant peal, and Alice with some disturbance of manner came out and announced that a gentleman wished to see the master. Darnell went into the drawing-room, where Alice had lit one gas so that it flared and burnt with a rushing sound, and in this distorting light there waited a stout, elderly gentleman, whose countenance was altogether unknown to him. He stared blankly, and hesitated, about to speak, but the visitor began.

“You don’t know who I am, but I expect you’ll know my name. It’s Nixon.”

He did not wait to be interrupted. He sat down and plunged into narrative, and after the first few words, Darnell, whose mind was not altogether unprepared, listened without much astonishment.

“And the long and the short of it is,” Mr. Nixon said at last, “she’s gone stark, staring mad, and we had to put her away today﻿—poor thing.”

His voice broke a little, and he wiped his eyes hastily, for though stout and successful he was not unfeeling, and he was fond of his wife. He had spoken quickly, and had gone lightly over many details which might have interested specialists in certain kinds of mania, and Darnell was sorry for his evident distress.

“I came here,” he went on after a brief pause, “because I found out she had been to see you last Sunday, and I knew the sort of story she must have told.”

Darnell showed him the prophetic leaflet which Mrs. Nixon had dropped in the garden. “Did you know about this?” he said.

===Chinese===

这一切的记忆，随着他年岁长大、庸常人生的锁链在他灵魂上铆紧，早已褪色、成了影子；他四围的空气，对这类念头几乎致命；只有偶尔半意识的时刻或在睡梦里，他才重访那远西的谷地——那里，风的呼吸是一篇咒文，每一片叶、每一条溪、每一座山都诉说着重大而不可言说的奥秘。可如今，破碎的异象大半归还了他；他含爱望着妻子的眼睛，看见静谧森林里水潭的微光，看见傍晚升起的雾，听见蜿蜒河流的音乐。

那是尼克松太太那次古怪而半被忘却的来访开头的一周的星期五傍晚，两人正这样坐着，门铃猛响了一声刺耳的调子，达内尔正懊恼，爱丽丝神色慌张地出来说，有位先生要见主人。达内尔走进客厅——爱丽丝点了一盏煤气灯，火苗呼呼地烧得发亮——在这歪曲变形的光里等着一位胖胖的上了年纪的先生，那张脸他全然不识。他茫然瞪着，迟疑着正要开口，来客先说了。

“你不认得我，不过我想你认得我的名字。我是尼克松。”

他不等别人打断。他坐下来，一头扎进叙述；开头几句一过，达内尔——他心里并非毫无准备——便听着，不怎么吃惊了。

“长话短说，”尼克松先生末了说，“她是彻底疯了，疯得眼睁睁的，咱们今天只好把她送走了——可怜的人。”

他的声音有点哑，慌忙擦了擦眼睛；因为他人虽发福、虽发达，却不是没心肝的，他也是疼他妻子的。他讲得很快，好些也许会让专治某类疯病的行家感兴趣的细节，他都轻轻带过；达内尔为他显然的痛苦很难过。

“我到这儿来，”他略停了停又接着说，“因为我查出她上个星期天来过你们这儿；我猜得着她准讲了些什么样的故事。”

达内尔把尼克松太太掉在园子里的那张预言传单拿给他看。“这个你知道吗？”他说。

===Original===

“Oh, *him*,” said the old man, with some approach to cheerfulness; “oh yes, I thrashed *him* black and blue the day before yesterday.”

“Isn’t he mad? Who is the man?”

“He’s not mad, he’s bad. He’s a little Welsh skunk named Richards. He’s been running some sort of chapel over at New Barnet for the last few years, and my poor wife﻿—she never could find the parish church good enough for her﻿—had been going to his damned schism shop for the last twelvemonth. It was all that finished her off. Yes; I thrashed *him* the day before yesterday, and I’m not afraid of a summons either. I know him, and he knows I know him.”

Old Nixon whispered something in Darnell’s ear, and chuckled faintly as he repeated for the third time his formula﻿—

“I thrashed *him* black and blue the day before yesterday.”

Darnell could only murmur condolences and express his hope that Mrs. Nixon might recover.

The old man shook his head.

“I’m afraid there’s no hope of that,” he said. “I’ve had the best advice, but they couldn’t do anything, and told me so.”

Presently he asked to see his niece, and Darnell went out and prepared Mary as well as he could. She could scarcely take in the news that her aunt was a hopeless maniac, for Mrs. Nixon, having been extremely stupid all her days, had naturally succeeded in passing with her relations as typically sensible. With the Reynolds family, as with the great majority of us, want of imagination is always equated with sanity, and though many of us have never heard of Lombroso we are his ready-made converts. We have always believed that poets are mad, and if statistics unfortunately show that few poets have really been inhabitants of lunatic asylums, it is soothing to learn that nearly all poets have had whooping-cough, which is doubtless, like intoxication, a minor madness.

“But is it really true?” she asked at length. “Are you certain uncle is not deceiving you? Aunt seemed so sensible always.”

She was helped at last by recollecting that Aunt Marian used to get up very early of mornings, and then they went into the drawing-room and talked to the old man. His evident kindliness and honesty grew upon Mary, in spite of a lingering belief in her aunt’s fables, and when he left, it was with a promise to come to see them again.

===Chinese===

“哦，他呀，”老头说，透出几分轻快；“哦，知道，前天我把他揍了个乌青透亮。”

“他不疯吗？那人是谁？”

“他不疯，他坏。他是个姓理查兹的威尔士小无赖。这几年他在新巴尼特那边开着个什么小礼拜堂；我那可怜的太太——她一向嫌教区教堂不够好——这一年里全上他那个分裂派的破铺子去。把她断送的就是这个。是了；前天我把他揍了个乌青透亮，传票我也不怕。我知道他的底，他知道我知道。”

老尼克松凑在达内尔耳边咕哝了一句，把他的老调重弹到第三遍，还轻轻笑了——

“前天我把他揍了个乌青透亮。”

达内尔只能低声慰问几句，说一声盼望尼克松太太能康复。

老头摇了摇头。

===Original===

Mrs. Darnell said she felt tired, and went to bed; and Darnell returned to the garden and began to pace to and fro, collecting his thoughts. His immeasurable relief at the intelligence that, after all, Mrs. Nixon was not coming to live with them taught him that, despite his submission, his dread of the event had been very great. The weight was removed, and now he was free to consider his life without reference to the grotesque intrusion that he had feared. He sighed for joy, and as he paced to and fro he savoured the scent of the night, which, though it came faintly to him in that brick-bound suburb, summoned to his mind across many years the odour of the world at night as he had known it in that short sojourn of his boyhood; the odour that rose from the earth when the flame of the sun had gone down beyond the mountain, and the afterglow had paled in the sky and on the fields. And as he recovered as best he could these lost dreams of an enchanted land, there came to him other images of his childhood, forgotten and yet not forgotten, dwelling unheeded in dark places of the memory, but ready to be summoned forth. He remembered one fantasy that had long haunted him. As he lay half asleep in the forest on one hot afternoon of that memorable visit to the country, he had “made believe” that a little companion had come to him out of the blue mists and the green light beneath the leaves﻿—a white girl with long black hair, who had played with him and whispered her secrets in his ear, as his father lay sleeping under a tree; and from that summer afternoon, day by day, she had been beside him; she had visited him in the wilderness of London, and even in recent years there had come to him now and again the sense of her presence, in the midst of the heat and turmoil of the City. The last visit he remembered well; it was a few weeks before he married, and from the depths of some futile task he had looked up with puzzled eyes, wondering why the close air suddenly grew scented with green leaves, why the murmur of the trees and the wash of the river on the reeds came to his ears; and then that sudden rapture to which he had given a name and an individuality possessed him utterly.

===Chinese===

“怕是没有指望了，”他说。“名医用遍了，他们没有法子，也对我明说了。”

过了一会儿，他要见见玛丽；达内尔出去，尽其所能给玛丽打了招呼。姨妈成了个没救的疯子，这消息她一时咽不下，因为尼克松太太一辈子蠢得出奇，自然在亲戚里落下了个明白人的名声。在雷诺兹家，也同我们大多数人一样，缺乏想象力永远被当作神智健全；我们许多人虽从没听说过龙勃罗梭，却都是他现成的信徒。我们一向相信诗人是疯子；倘若统计不幸表明，住进疯人院的诗人其实寥寥，那么得知几乎所有的诗人都害过百日咳，也就足以宽怀了——百日咳，想来同醉酒一样，是一种轻性的疯狂。

“可这是真的吗？”她终于问，“你肯定叔叔没有骗你？姨妈一向那么明白。”

末了帮她转过弯来的，是想起玛丽安姨妈从前每天清早起床；随后他们进了客厅，同老人交谈。他那显然的厚道与诚实渐渐赢得了玛丽的心，尽管她对姨妈的种种奇谈还存着几分信；他临走时答应再来看他们。

===Original===

He knew then how the dull flesh of man can be like fire; and now, looking back from a new standpoint on this and other experiences, he realized how all that was real in his life had been unwelcomed, uncherished by him, had come to him, perhaps, in virtue of merely negative qualities on his part. And yet, as he reflected, he saw that there had been a chain of witnesses all through his life: again and again voices had whispered in his ear words in a strange language that he now recognized as his native tongue; the common street had not been lacking in visions of the true land of his birth; and in all the passing and repassing of the world he saw that there had been emissaries ready to guide his feet on the way of the great journey.

A week or two after the visit of Mr. Nixon, Darnell took his annual holiday.

===Chinese===

达内尔太太说乏了，上床去了；达内尔回到园里，来回踱步，收敛心神。听到尼克松太太到底不来同住的消息，他松了那样一大口气，这才明白，自己尽管顺从了，先前对这件事的惧怕有多深。重担卸下了；如今他可以无所牵挂地考量自己的生活，不必再顾虑他害怕过的那场怪诞的侵扰。他欢喜得叹了口气；踱来踱去，他品味着夜的气息——那气息虽在砖石封锁的郊区只淡淡传来，却隔着许多年，把他孩提时代那短短一居里熟识的夜世界的气味召上心头：那是太阳的火焰落到山后、余晖在天空和田野上褪尽之后，从大地上升起的气味。他正尽力拾回这些失落的迷境之梦，童年别的影像也来了——忘了，又没全忘，蛰居在记忆的暗处，只待一声召唤。他想起一个久已纠缠他的幻想。在那次难忘的乡居里，一个炎热的午后，他半睡半醒躺在林中，“假装”有一个小小的伴侣从蓝雾和树叶底下的绿光里向他走来——一个黑长发的白姑娘，同他玩耍，在他耳边说她的秘密，那时他父亲正在树下酣睡；从那个夏日的午后起，日复一日，她都在他身边；她到伦敦的荒漠里来看他；甚至近年来，在城里的燥热与喧嚣当中，他也不时觉出她的临在。最后一次他记得清楚：那是在他婚前几个星期，他从一桩劳而无功的活计深处抬起茫然的眼，纳闷闷热的空气里怎么突然有了绿叶的清香，怎么耳朵里来了树的低语和河水拍打芦苇的声响；随后，那个他曾赋以名字和性灵的、突如其来的狂喜，完完全全占有了他。

===Original===

There was no question of Walton-on-the-Naze, or of anything of the kind, as he quite agreed with his wife’s longing for some substantial sum put by against the evil day. But the weather was still fine, and he lounged away the time in his garden beneath the tree, or he sauntered out on long aimless walks in the western purlieus of London, not unvisited by that old sense of some great ineffable beauty, concealed by the dim and dingy veils of grey interminable streets. Once, on a day of heavy rain he went to the “box-room,” and began to turn over the papers in the old hair trunk﻿—scraps and odds and ends of family history, some of them in his father’s handwriting, others in faded ink, and there were a few ancient pocketbooks, filled with manuscript of a still earlier time, and in these the ink was glossier and blacker than any writing fluids supplied by stationers of later days. Darnell had hung up the portrait of the ancestor in this room, and had bought a solid kitchen table and a chair; so that Mrs. Darnell, seeing him looking over his old documents, half thought of naming the room “Mr. Darnell’s study.” He had not glanced at these relics of his family for many years, but from the hour when the rainy morning sent him to them, he remained constant to research till the end of the holidays. It was a new interest, and he began to fashion in his mind a faint picture of his forefathers, and of their life in that grey old house in the river valley, in the western land of wells and streams and dark and ancient woods. And there were stranger things than mere notes on family history amongst that odd litter of old disregarded papers, and when he went back to his work in the City some of the men fancied that he was in some vague manner changed in appearance; but he only laughed when they asked him where he had been and what he had been doing with himself. But Mary noticed that every evening he spent at least an hour in the box-room; she was rather sorry at the waste of time involved in reading old papers about dead people. And one afternoon, as they were out together on a somewhat dreary walk towards Acton, Darnell stopped at a hopeless secondhand bookshop, and after scanning the rows of shabby books in the window, went in and purchased two volumes.

===Chinese===

那时他就懂得了，人这块迟钝的肉怎能像火一样；如今，从一个新的立足点上回顾这一桩桩经历，他明白了：他一生中真正真实的东西，从来没有被他欢迎、被他珍视过；它们临到他，或许恰恰是凭他身上那些纯粹消极的品质。然而，他一转念，看见这一生里原有一整条见证人的链环：一而再、再而三，有声音在他耳边用一种奇怪的语言低语，如今他认出来了，那是他的母语；寻常的街道上，也从不曾缺少他真正故乡之地的异象；在世事的来来往往中，他看出处处有使者随时预备，好引他的脚走上那伟大旅程的路。

尼克松先生来访后一两个星期，达内尔休了他的年假。

沃尔顿昂纳齐是谈不上了，那一类的想头都谈不上，因为他完全赞成妻子的心愿，要攒下一笔实在的款项以备不虞。可天气还晴好，他就在园里树下消磨时光，或者荡出去，在伦敦西郊做长长的无目的的散步，那古老的、某个莫可名状的至美之感，也不时光顾他——那美就藏在灰色望不到头的街道那些昏暗破败的帷幕后面。有一回，一个豪雨的日子，他去了“杂物间”，动手翻检那只旧发皮箱里的纸片——家史的零笺断简，有几张是他父亲的手笔，别的是褪了色的墨迹；还有几本更早年代的手写小册子，那里的墨水比后世文具店供应的任何墨水都更黑亮。达内尔已经把那位先祖的画像挂在这间屋里，还买了一张结实的厨房桌和一把椅子；达内尔太太见他整理旧文书，半想把这间屋子命名作“达内尔先生的书房”。这些家传的故纸，他多年没有正眼瞧过；可从那个落雨的早晨把他打发去的时辰起，直到假期结束，他潜心其间，再没有间断。这是个新的兴味；他开始在脑子里给列祖列宗拼出一幅淡淡的图画，画出他们在河谷里那座灰老宅中的生活——在那片有泉有溪、有黑古树林的西土上。而在那堆无人理会的故纸烂片中，还有比家史笔记更奇怪的东西；等他回城里上班，有些人觉得他模样里有种说不清的变化；可他们问他去了哪儿、干了些什么，他只一笑。玛丽倒留意到，他每天晚上至少要在杂物间消磨一个钟头；读这些讲死人的旧纸，费这些工夫，她颇觉可惜。一天下午，两人一同往阿克顿方向做一次颇沉闷的散步，达内尔在一家没有指望的旧书铺前站住，把橱窗里一排排寒碜的书扫了一眼，进去买了两册。

===Original===

They proved to be a Latin dictionary and grammar, and she was surprised to hear her husband declare his intention of acquiring the Latin language.

===Chinese===

原来是一部拉丁文词典和一部文法；听丈夫宣布自己有意学会拉丁文，她吃了一惊。

===Original===

But, indeed, all his conduct impressed her as indefinably altered; and she began to be a little alarmed, though she could scarcely have formed her fears in words. But she knew that in some way that was all indefined and beyond the grasp of her thought their lives had altered since the summer, and no single thing wore quite the same aspect as before. If she looked out into the dull street with its rare loiterers, it was the same and yet it had altered, and if she opened the window in the early morning the wind that entered came with a changed breath that spoke some message that she could not understand. And day by day passed by in the old course, and not even the four walls were altogether familiar, and the voices of men and women sounded with strange notes, with the echo, rather, of a music that came over unknown hills. And day by day as she went about her household work, passing from shop to shop in those dull streets that were a network, a fatal labyrinth of grey desolation on every side, there came to her sense half-seen images of some other world, as if she walked in a dream, and every moment must bring her to light and to awakening, when the grey should fade, and regions long desired should appear in glory. Again and again it seemed as if that which was hidden would be shown even to the sluggish testimony of sense; and as she went to and fro from street to street of that dim and weary suburb, and looked on those grey material walls, they seemed as if a light glowed behind them, and again and again the mystic fragrance of incense was blown to her nostrils from across the verge of that world which is not so much impenetrable as ineffable, and to her ears came the dream of a chant that spoke of hidden choirs about all her ways. She struggled against these impressions, refusing her assent to the testimony of them, since all the pressure of credited opinion for three hundred years has been directed towards stamping out real knowledge, and so effectually has this been accomplished that we can only recover the truth through much anguish. And so Mary passed the days in a strange perturbation, clinging to common things and common thoughts, as if she feared that one morning she would wake up in an unknown world to a changed life.

===Chinese===

可不是，他的一举一动在她眼里都有了说不出的改变；她开始有点惊慌，虽说她的惧怕还难以形诸言语。可她知道，自入夏以来，他们的生活不知怎么变了——全然未加界定、超出她思想的把握；没有一样东西还全然是从前的面目。她若望一望那条行人寥寥的沉闷街道，它既是原样，又变了样；她若清晨推开窗，进来的风便带着换了意味的呼吸，捎来一句她听不懂的口信。日子照旧一天天过去，可连这四堵墙也不全然亲切了；男男女女的声音里响着陌生的调子——不如说，回响着一种翻过生山而来的音乐。日复一日，她操持家务，在那些沉闷街道上一家铺子一家铺子地走——那些街结成一张网，四面八方一座灰色的、致命的迷宫——她的感官便有半个影像的另一个世界到来，仿佛她行在梦中，随时都要天光大亮、梦醒过来，灰色褪尽，渴慕已久的疆域将在荣光里显现。一次又一次，那隐藏着的似乎就要向迟钝感官的见证敞开；她在那昏沉倦怠的郊区里从一条街走到另一条街，望着那些灰色的、物质的墙，墙后仿佛有光在燃；一次又一次，神秘熏香的芬芳越过那个世界的边涯吹进她的鼻孔——那世界与其说不可穿透，不如说不可言说；她耳边传来一支圣咏的梦，讲说着她一路四围隐藏的唱诗班。她抗拒这些印象，不肯应允它们作证，因为三百年来，一切有信用舆论的压力，都朝着扑灭真知识的方向使去，而且收效如此彻底，我们唯有经过许多苦痛才能把真理收复。就这样，玛丽在奇异的激动不安里度着日子，紧紧抱住寻常的事物、寻常的念头，仿佛害怕哪个早晨醒来，自己已在一个未知的世界里、过上了一种全然改变的生活。

===Original===

And Edward Darnell went day by day to his labour and returned in the evening, always with that shining of light within his eyes and upon his face, with the gaze of wonder that was greater day by day, as if for him the veil grew thin and soon would disappear.

From these great matters both in herself and in her husband Mary shrank back, afraid, perhaps, that if she began the question the answer might be too wonderful. She rather taught herself to be troubled over little things; she asked herself what attraction there could be in the old records over which she supposed Edward to be poring night after night in the cold room upstairs. She had glanced over the papers at Darnell’s invitation, and could see but little interest in them; there were one or two sketches, roughly done in pen and ink, of the old house in the west: it looked a shapeless and fantastic place, furnished with strange pillars and stranger ornaments on the projecting porch; and on one side a roof dipped down almost to the earth, and in the centre there was something that might almost be a tower rising above the rest of the building. Then there were documents that seemed all names and dates, with here and there a coat of arms done in the margin, and she came upon a string of uncouth Welsh names linked together by the word *ap* in a chain that looked endless. There was a paper covered with signs and figures that meant nothing to her, and then there were the pocketbooks, full of old-fashioned writing, and much of it in Latin, as her husband told her﻿—it was a collection as void of significance as a treatise on conic sections, so far as Mary was concerned. But night after night Darnell shut himself up with the musty rolls, and more than ever when he rejoined her he bore upon his face the blazonry of some great adventure. And one night she asked him what interested him so much in the papers he had shown her.

===Chinese===

而爱德华·达内尔日复一日去上他的工，傍晚归来，眼里、脸上总带着那道内在的光，那目中的惊奇与日俱增，仿佛在他，那层帷幕越来越薄，转眼就要消失。

这些在她自己、在她丈夫身上的大事，玛丽都退缩着不敢近前；她或许害怕，她若起了问，那答案会奇得受不住。她宁可教自己为小事烦心；她问自己，楼上那间冷屋子里，爱德华夜夜埋头的那堆旧记录，到底有什么迷人处。应达内尔之邀，她浏览过那些纸片，看不出多大意味：有一两幅钢笔草图，粗粗画着西边那座老宅：看去是个没形没样、光怪陆离的地方，凸出的门廊上立着古怪的柱子、更古怪的雕饰；一侧的屋顶几乎垂到地面，当中有个什么，几乎算得一座塔，高出其余的屋身。再往下是些似乎全是名字与年月的文书，页边这里那里画着一枚纹章；她还撞见一长串粗野的威尔士名字，用“阿普”（ap）一词串成一环，看着没有尽头。有一张纸画满记号和数字，于她毫无意义；再有便是那些小册子，写满老式笔画，多半是拉丁文，她丈夫告诉她的——就玛丽而言，这一收藏之空洞无谓，不下于一部论圆锥曲线的论文。可达内尔夜复一夜把自己关在霉味卷帙间，同她重聚时，脸上比以往更带着某场大冒险的纹章。一夜，她问他，他给她看的那些纸里，到底什么叫他这样入迷。

===Original===

He was delighted with the question. Somehow they had not talked much together for the last few weeks, and he began to tell her of the records of the old race from which he came, of the old strange house of grey stone between the forest and the river. The family went back and back, he said, far into the dim past, beyond the Normans, beyond the Saxons, far into the Roman days, and for many hundred years they had been petty kings, with a strong fortress high up on the hill, in the heart of the forest; and even now the great mounds remained, whence one could look through the trees towards the mountain on one side and across the yellow sea on the other. The real name of the family was not Darnell; that was assumed by one Iolo ap Taliesin ap Iorwerth in the sixteenth century﻿—why, Darnell did not seem to understand. And then he told her how the race had dwindled in prosperity, century by century, till at last there was nothing left but the grey house and a few acres of land bordering the river.

“And do you know, Mary,” he said, “I suppose we shall go and live there some day or other. My great-uncle, who has the place now, made money in business when he was a young man, and I believe he will leave it all to me. I know I am the only relation he has. How strange it would be. What a change from the life here.”

“You never told me that. Don’t you think your great-uncle might leave his house and his money to somebody he knows really well? You haven’t seen him since you were a little boy, have you?”

“No; but we write once a year. And from what I have heard my father say, I am sure the old man would never leave the house out of the family. Do you think you would like it?”

“I don’t know. Isn’t it very lonely?”

===Chinese===

这问题叫他高兴。不知怎么，近几个星期他们不大交谈了；他开始对她讲起他那一族的记录，讲起森林与河流之间那座灰色古宅的旧事。他说，这个家世上溯再上溯，一直伸进朦胧的远古，越过诺曼人，越过撒克逊人，远远伸进罗马人的岁月；几百年间，他们是称孤道寡的小王，在森林腹地的高山上有一座坚固的堡垒；直到如今，巨大的土垒还在，从那儿可以透过树隙，一侧望向大山，一侧望过黄色的海。这个家的真姓本来不是达内尔；那是十六世纪一个叫约洛·阿普·塔列辛·阿普·约沃思的人改用的——为什么，达内尔似乎也不明白。接着他告诉她，这一族的运势怎样一世纪一世纪衰落下来，到末了，只剩那座灰宅和临河的几英亩地。

“你知道吗，玛丽，”他说，“我想咱们早晚要搬去那儿住。如今管着那地方的是我的叔祖父，他年轻时做生意挣了钱；我相信他会把这一切都留给我。我知道我是他唯一的亲眷。那该多奇怪啊。同这儿的生活隔得多远。”

“这事你从没告诉过我。你不觉得，你那位叔祖父兴许会把房子和钱留给一个他真正知根知底的人？你从做小孩子起就没见过他，对不对？”

“对；可我们一年通一回信。而且从我听父亲说的来看，我敢保老人家绝不会叫房产出了姓。你说你会喜欢那儿吗？”

“不知道。那儿不是很冷清吗？”

===Original===

“I suppose it is. I forget whether there are any other houses in sight, but I don’t think there are any at all near. But what a change! No City, no streets, no people passing to and fro; only the sound of the wind and the sight of the green leaves and the green hills, and the song of the voices of the earth.”﻿ ﻿… He checked himself suddenly, as if he feared that he was about to tell some secret that must not yet be uttered; and indeed, as he spoke of the change from the little street in Shepherd’s Bush to that ancient house in the woods of the far west, a change seemed already to possess himself, and his voice put on the modulation of an antique chant. Mary looked at him steadily and touched his arm, and he drew a long breath before he spoke again.

“It is the old blood calling to the old land,” he said. “I was forgetting that I am a clerk in the City.”

It was, doubtless, the old blood that had suddenly stirred in him; the resurrection of the old spirit that for many centuries had been faithful to secrets that are now disregarded by most of us, that now day by day was quickened more and more in his heart, and grew so strong that it was hard to conceal. He was indeed almost in the position of the man in the tale, who, by a sudden electric shock, lost the vision of the things about him in the London streets, and gazed instead upon the sea and shore of an island in the Antipodes; for Darnell only clung with an effort to the interests and the atmosphere which, till lately, had seemed all the world to him; and the grey house and the wood and the river, symbols of the other sphere, intruded as it were into the landscape of the London suburb.

===Chinese===

“我想是冷清。我记不真切望得望不得见别的房子，我想附近一所也没有。可想想这变化！没有城，没有街，没有来来往往的人；只有风声，只有绿叶青山入眼，只有大地的众声歌唱。”……他猛然收住，像怕就要说出一个还不许说的秘密；说实在的，当他讲着从谢泼德布什的小街到远西树林中那座古宅的变化时，那变化仿佛已经占有了他本人，他的嗓音换上了古歌的调门。玛丽定定地望着他，碰了碰他的手臂；再开口前，他深深吸了一口气。

“是古老的血在呼唤古老的土地，”他说，“我忘了我是个城里的职员。”

无疑，是古老的血在他身上骤然涌动；是古老的魂灵复活了——许多世纪以来，它始终忠守着我们今日大多数人弃如敝屣的奥秘；如今它在他心里一天比一天活跃，长得这样壮盛，几乎掩藏不住。他当真近乎传说里那个人的境地：那人遭了一次电击，伦敦街上眼前之物的视觉没有了，看见的却是地球另一面一座岛的海滨；因为达内尔只是勉力抓着那些近来以前还当作全世界的营求与氛围；而灰宅、树林、河流——另一个世界的象征——竟仿佛挤进了伦敦郊区的风景里来。

===Original===

But he went on, with more restraint, telling his stories of far-off ancestors, how one of them, the most remote of all, was called a saint, and was supposed to possess certain mysterious secrets often alluded to in the papers as the “Hidden Songs of Iolo Sant.” And then with an abrupt transition he recalled memories of his father and of the strange, shiftless life in dingy lodgings in the backwaters of London, of the dim stucco streets that were his first recollections, of forgotten squares in North London, and of the figure of his father, a grave bearded man who seemed always in a dream, as if he too sought for the vision of a land beyond the strong walls, a land where there were deep orchards and many shining hills, and fountains and water-pools gleaming under the leaves of the wood.

“I believe my father earned his living,” he went on, “such a living as he did earn, at the Record Office and the British Museum. He used to hunt up things for lawyers and country parsons who wanted old deeds inspected. He never made much, and we were always moving from one lodging to another﻿—always to out-of-the-way places where everything seemed to have run to seed. We never knew our neighbours﻿—we moved too often for that﻿—but my father had about half a dozen friends, elderly men like himself, who used to come to see us pretty often; and then, if there was any money, the lodging-house servant would go out for beer, and they would sit and smoke far into the night.

“I never knew much about these friends of his, but they all had the same look, the look of longing for something hidden. They talked of mysteries that I never understood, very little of their own lives, and when they did speak of ordinary affairs one could tell that they thought such matters as money and the want of it were unimportant trifles. When I grew up and went into the City, and met other young fellows and heard their way of talking, I wondered whether my father and his friends were not a little queer in their heads; but I know better now.”

===Chinese===

可他接着讲下去，多了几分节制，讲远祖的故事：他们中间有一个，最古最远的那一个，人称圣者，据说掌握着某些神秘的奥秘，纸片里屡屡称之为“伊洛圣者的隐藏之歌”。随后他陡然一转，忆起他的父亲，忆起伦敦背街暗巷昏暗客寓里那段清寒无着的岁月：他最早的记忆里那些灰泥小巷，北伦敦被遗忘的广场，还有父亲的身影——一个蓄须的严肃男人，仿佛永远在梦里，像他自己也在寻一个高墙之外的国度的异象：那国度里有深深的果园、许多发光的山，有泉水和水潭在树叶下闪亮。

“我相信父亲是挣饭吃的，”他接着说，“挣他挣到的那点饭，在档案局和不列颠博物馆。他替律师和乡下牧师查老契约。他从没挣到多少，我们老在搬家——从一处客寓搬到另一处——专搬那些偏僻地方，样样东西都像荒了蔫了。邻居我们从来不认识——搬得太勤——可父亲有六来个朋友，同他一样的上了年纪的人，常来看我们；手头宽裕的时候，客寓的女仆就出去打啤酒，他们坐着抽烟，直抽到深夜。

“这些朋友我始终不大了解，可他们全是一副面目，一副渴慕着什么隐藏之物的面目。他们谈的奥秘我不懂，很少谈他们自己的生活；偶尔谈起俗务，也听得出，他们把钱、把钱荒这类事看作不要紧的碎屑。等我长大进了城，见识了别的年轻人、听了他们谈天，我疑心父亲和他那些朋友脑子都有点毛病；如今我明白了。”

===Original===

So night after night Darnell talked to his wife, seeming to wander aimlessly from the dingy lodging-houses, where he had spent his boyhood in the company of his father and the other seekers, to the old house hidden in that far western valley, and the old race that had so long looked at the setting of the sun over the mountain. But in truth there was one end in all that he spoke, and Mary felt that beneath his words, however indifferent they might seem, there was hidden a purpose, that they were to embark on a great and marvellous adventure.

===Chinese===

就这样，达内尔夜复一夜对妻子讲着，仿佛信步从那些昏暗的客寓——他在那里同父亲和别的寻访者度过了少年——走到藏在那远西山谷里的老宅，走到那样长久凝望太阳落山的古族。可他说的一切，其实只有一个归宿；玛丽觉出他的言语底下，无论显得多么平淡，藏着一个目的：他们将要启程，去做一场伟大而奇妙的冒险。

===Original===

So day by day the world became more magical; day by day the work of separation was being performed, the gross accidents were being refined away. Darnell neglected no instruments that might be useful in the work; and now he neither lounged at home on Sunday mornings, nor did he accompany his wife to the Gothic blasphemy which pretended to be a church. They had discovered a little church of another fashion in a back street, and Darnell, who had found in one of the old notebooks the maxim *Incredibilia sola Credenda*, soon perceived how high and glorious a thing was that service at which he assisted. Our stupid ancestors taught us that we could become wise by studying books on “science,” by meddling with test-tubes, geological specimens, microscopic preparations, and the like; but they who have cast off these follies know that they must read not “science” books, but mass-books, and that the soul is made wise by the contemplation of mystic ceremonies and elaborate and curious rites. In such things Darnell found a wonderful mystery language, which spoke at once more secretly and more directly than the formal creeds; and he saw that, in a sense, the whole world is but a great ceremony or sacrament, which teaches under visible forms a hidden and transcendent doctrine. It was thus that he found in the ritual of the church a perfect image of the world; an image purged, exalted, and illuminate, a holy house built up of shining and translucent stones, in which the burning torches were more significant than the wheeling stars, and the fuming incense was a more certain token than the rising of the mist. His soul went forth with the albed procession in its white and solemn order, the mystic dance that signifies rapture and a joy above all joys, and when he beheld Love slain and rise again victorious he knew that he witnessed, in a figure, the consummation of all things, the Bridal of all Bridals, the mystery that is beyond all mysteries, accomplished from the foundation of the world. So day by day the house of his life became more magical.

===Chinese===

就这样，世界一天比一天更有魔法；一天一天，分离的功行在进行，粗浊的偶然正被炼净。凡是对功行有用的器具，达内尔一样不废；如今他星期天早晨既不再赖在家里，也不陪妻子去那座冒称教堂的哥特渎神物了。他们在一条后街寻着一座别样面目的小教堂；达内尔在一册旧笔记里读到过一句格言，Incredibilia sola Credenda（唯不可思议者方为可信），不久便看出，他在那礼拜中所参与的，是怎样一件高华荣耀的大事。我们那些愚蠢的祖先教导我们，说读“科学”书、摆弄试管、地质标本、显微切片等等，就能变聪明；可凡是甩脱了这些蠢行的都知道：要读的不是“科学”书，而是弥撒书；灵魂是靠观想神秘的仪礼、繁缛而奇巧的圣仪才变聪明的。达内尔在这些事物里发现了一种奇妙的奥秘语言，比正统的信经说得更隐秘也更直接；他看出，在某种意义上，整个世界不过是一场大典礼或一件大圣事，借可见的形式传授一套隐秘而超越的教义。他就是这样在教会的礼文中找到了世界的一幅完美图像：一幅炼净了的、高举了的、照亮了的图像，一座以发光半透的石头筑成的圣殿——在那里，燃烧的火炬比轮转的群星更有意义，氤氲的熏香比晨雾的升起更是确凿的凭证。他的灵魂随白衣的行列出发行进，在它白色庄严的秩序里，在那意味着狂喜、意味着超越一切欢喜之欢喜的神秘舞蹈里；当他眼见爱被杀而又凯旋复活，他知道，他以形象目击了万事的成全，万婚之婚，奥秘之上的奥秘，自创世之基便已成就。就这样，他生命之宅一天比一天更有魔法。

===Original===

And at the same time he began to guess that if in the New Life there are new and unheard-of joys, there are also new and unheard-of dangers. In his manuscript books which professed to deliver the outer sense of those mysterious “Hidden Songs of Iolo Sant” there was a little chapter that bore the heading: *Fons Sacer non in communem Usum convertendus est*, and by diligence, with much use of the grammar and dictionary, Darnell was able to construe the by no means complex Latin of his ancestor. The special book which contained the chapter in question was one of the most singular in the collection, since it bore the title *Terra de Iolo*, and on the surface, with an ingenious concealment of its real symbolism, it affected to give an account of the orchards, fields, woods, roads, tenements, and waterways in the possession of Darnell’s ancestors. Here, then, he read of the Holy Well, hidden in the Wistman’s Wood﻿—*Sylva Sapientum*﻿—“a fountain of abundant water, which no heats of summer can ever dry, which no flood can ever defile, which is as a water of life, to them that thirst for life, a stream of cleansing to them that would be pure, and a medicine of such healing virtue that by it, through the might of God and the intercession of His saints, the most grievous wounds are made whole.” But the water of this well was to be kept sacred perpetually, it was not to be used for any common purpose, nor to satisfy any bodily thirst; but ever to be esteemed as holy, “even as the water which the priest hath hallowed.” And in the margin a comment in a later hand taught Darnell something of the meaning of these prohibitions. He was warned not to use the Well of Life as a mere luxury of mortal life, as a new sensation, as a means of making the insipid cup of everyday existence more palatable. “For,” said the commentator, “we are not called to sit as the spectators in a theatre, there to watch the play performed before us, but we are rather summoned to stand in the very scene itself, and there fervently to enact our parts in a great and wonderful mystery.”

===Chinese===

与此同时，他开始猜到：新生命里既有未之前闻的新欢喜，也有未之前闻的新危险。在他那些声称传达“伊洛圣者隐藏之歌”外义的手册里，有一小章，标题是：Fons Sacer non in communem Usum convertendus est（圣泉不得移作俗用）；凭着勤勉，多翻文法和词典，达内尔把他祖先那远非艰深的拉丁文读通了。载有此章的那册书，是全收藏里最奇特的几册之一，书名 Terra de Iolo（《伊洛之地》），表面上匠心独运地藏起它真正的象征，装作记述达内尔祖上拥有的果园、田地、树林、道路、屋宇与水道。就在这里，他读到那口藏在智者林（Sylva Sapientum）里的圣泉——“一泓丰沛的泉水，夏日之暑热永不能使之涸，洪水永不能使之浊；于渴慕生命者，它是生命之水；于愿求洁净者，它是涤罪之流；它又是那样一剂灵药，凭着上帝的大能与诸圣的转求，最沉重的创伤赖它得愈。”可这泉之水当永葆其圣，不得移作任何俗用，不得用来解肉身的渴；而当永远敬之为圣，“一如司铎祝圣过的水”。页边一条后人手笔的评注，教达内尔略略懂得了这些禁戒的用意。他受的警告是：不可把这生命之泉当作尘生的一种奢侈、一种新鲜刺激、一种给寡淡的日常杯盏添滋味的作料。“因为，”评注者说，“我们蒙召，不是坐在戏园里当看客，看戏在我们面前搬演；我们蒙召，是立到戏景的本身当中，在那里火热地扮演我们在一场伟大奇妙之奥秘里的角色。”

===Original===

Darnell could quite understand the temptation that was thus indicated. Though he had gone but a little way on the path, and had barely tested the over-runnings of that mystic well, he was already aware of the enchantment that was transmuting all the world about him, informing his life with a strange significance and romance. London seemed a city of the Arabian Nights, and its labyrinths of streets an enchanted maze; its long avenues of lighted lamps were as starry systems, and its immensity became for him an image of the endless universe. He could well imagine how pleasant it might be to linger in such a world as this, to sit apart and dream, beholding the strange pageant played before him; but the Sacred Well was not for common use, it was for the cleansing of the soul, and the healing of the grievous wounds of the spirit. There must be yet another transformation: London had become Bagdad; it must at last be transmuted to Syon, or in the phrase of one of his old documents, the City of the Cup.

===Chinese===

达内尔全然懂得这里所指的诱惑。他虽然才上路不远，不过浅尝了那神秘泉水的溢流，却已经觉出那正点化他四围世界的魔法，正使他的生活浸润着奇异的意趣与传奇。伦敦像一座《天方夜谭》的城，它街巷的迷宫是一座着了魔的迷阵；它一长列一长列的灯街如星汉系统，它的浩大之于他，成了无尽宇宙的一个形象。他全想得出，在这样一个世界里流连该多么惬意：袖手坐在一边做梦，看那奇异的盛典在面前搬演；可圣泉不是为俗用设的，它为的是灵魂的洁净、为的是医治心灵的创痕。还须有一次变化：伦敦已经成了巴格达；到末了，它必须被点化为锡安——或者，用他一册古文书里的话说，杯之城。

===Original===

And there were yet darker perils which the Iolo MSS. (as his father had named the collection) hinted at more or less obscurely. There were suggestions of an awful region which the soul might enter, of a transmutation that was unto death, of evocations which could summon the utmost forces of evil from their dark places﻿—in a word, of that sphere which is represented to most of us under the crude and somewhat childish symbolism of Black Magic. And here again he was not altogether without a dim comprehension of what was meant. He found himself recalling an odd incident that had happened long ago, which had remained all the years in his mind unheeded, amongst the many insignificant recollections of his childhood, and now rose before him, clear and distinct and full of meaning. It was on that memorable visit to the old house in the west, and the whole scene returned, with its smallest events, and the voices seemed to sound in his ears. It was a grey, still day of heavy heat that he remembered: he had stood on the lawn after breakfast, and wondered at the great peace and silence of the world. Not a leaf stirred in the trees on the lawn, not a whisper came from the myriad leaves of the wood; the flowers gave out sweet and heavy odours as if they breathed the dreams of the summer night; and far down the valley, the winding river was like dim silver under that dim and silvery sky, and the far hills and woods and fields vanished in the mist. The stillness of the air held him as with a charm; he leant all the morning against the rails that parted the lawn from the meadow, breathing the mystic breath of summer, and watching the fields brighten as with a sudden blossoming of shining flowers as the high mist grew thin for a moment before the hidden sun. As he watched thus, a man weary with heat, with some glance of horror in his eyes, passed him on his way to the house; but he stayed at his post till the old bell in the turret rang, and they dined all together, masters and servants, in the dark cool room that looked towards the still leaves of the wood. He could see that his uncle was upset about something, and when they had finished dinner he heard him tell his father that there was trouble at a farm; and it was settled that they should all drive over in the afternoon to some place with a strange name. But when the time came Mr.

===Chinese===

还有更暗的危险，伊洛文书（他父亲给这收藏起的名字）或明或暗地暗示过：有一个灵魂可能闯入的可怕境界；有一种趋于死亡的点化；有能把恶的极势从暗处唤出的召遣——一句话，就是那以粗糙而近乎孩子气的黑魔法象征、呈示在我们大多数人面前的领域。在这一点上，他也不是全然没有一分朦胧的领悟。他想起多年前发生过的一桩怪事，多年以来一直无人理会地躺在他心里，混在童年许许多多不要紧的回忆中间，如今却在他面前立起来，清楚、分明、充满意义。那正是在西边老宅那次难忘的做客；整个场景连同最小的事件都回来了，人声仿佛就在耳边。他记得的是灰暗无风的一天，暑气沉重：早饭后他立在草坪上，诧异于世界这伟大的安宁寂静。草坪上的树没有一片叶子动，树林里万千的叶没有一声低语；花吐出甜而浓的香气，仿佛呼吸着夏夜的梦；远远的谷底，蜿蜒的河在灰银色的天穹下像暗银，远山、树林、田野都消失在雾里。空气的静定像符咒一样攫住了他；一整个早晨他都倚在隔开草坪与草场的栅栏上，呼吸夏日的神秘气息，看高处的雾在隐藏的太阳前稀薄片刻时，田野怎样像忽然开出一片闪光的花而亮起来。他这样望着，一个热得困乏的男人走过去往宅子去，眼里带一点惊惶；可孩子仍旧守着岗位，直到塔楼里的老钟敲响，主仆一同在那间朝着树林沉静枝叶的阴凉屋子里吃饭。他看得出叔叔为什么事心烦；饭罢，他听见叔叔告诉父亲，一个田庄出了乱子；于是议定下午他们全体赶车去一个名字古怪的地方。可到时候，老达内尔

===Original===

Darnell was too deep in old books and tobacco smoke to be stirred from his corner, and Edward and his uncle went alone in the dogcart. They drove swiftly down the narrow lane, into the road that followed the winding river, and crossed the bridge at Caermaen by the mouldering Roman walls, and then, skirting the deserted, echoing village, they came out on a broad white turnpike road, and the limestone dust followed them like a cloud. Then, suddenly, they turned to the north by such a road as Edward had never seen before. It was so narrow that there was barely room for the cart to pass, and the footway was of rock, and the banks rose high above them as they slowly climbed the long, steep way, and the untrimmed hedges on either side shut out the light. And the ferns grew thick and green upon the banks, and hidden wells dripped down upon them; and the old man told him how the lane in winter was a torrent of swirling water, so that no one could pass by it. On they went, ascending and then again descending, always in that deep hollow under the wild woven boughs, and the boy wondered vainly what the country was like on either side. And now the air grew darker, and the hedge on one bank was but the verge of a dark and rustling wood, and the grey limestone rocks had changed to dark-red earth flecked with green patches and veins of marl, and suddenly in the stillness from the depths of the wood a bird began to sing a melody that charmed the heart into another world, that sang to the child’s soul of the blessed faery realm beyond the woods of the earth, where the wounds of man are healed. And so at last, after many turnings and windings, they came to a high bare land where the lane broadened out into a kind of common, and along the edge of this place there were scattered three or four old cottages, and one of them was a little tavern. Here they stopped, and a man came out and tethered the tired horse to a post and gave him water; and old Mr. Darnell took the child’s hand and led him by a path across the fields.

===Chinese===

先生在旧书和烟草的浓雾里陷得太深，不肯从他的角落里挪动，爱德华便同叔父单独坐双轮轻便马车去了。马车飞快驶下窄巷，上了沿蜿蜒河流的大路，在凯尔曼越过桥，傍着倾颓的罗马残墙；随后绕过那座空寂回声的村子，驶上一条宽阔的白色收费路，石灰岩的尘土像云一样跟着他们。随后，忽然，他们折向北，走上爱德华从未见过的一条路。路窄得马车几乎错不开身，路肩是岩石的，两岸高高拔起在头顶，长长的陡坡慢慢爬着，两边不加修剪的树篱把天光都遮断了。蕨在两岸长得又密又绿，隐秘的泉涓涓滴在他们身上；老人告诉他，这巷子一到冬天便是一道打着旋的激流，谁也休想通行。他们一路走，升上去又降下来，始终在那野枝交织的深洼底下，孩子白费劲地猜想两岸该是什么乡野。后来空气暗下来，一面岸上的树篱只是一片幽暗喧响的树林的边涯，灰色的石灰岩换成了暗红土，斑斑绿意和大理石纹般的泥灰；忽然，在寂静里，从树林深处，一只鸟唱起一支曲子，把心引进另一个世界，向孩子的灵魂唱着树林之外那蒙福的仙乡，在那里，人的创伤得着医治。就这样，转过许多弯，绕过许多坡，他们终于到了一片高而裸露的土地，巷子在那里宽展开来，成了一片公地；沿这片地的边上，散落着三四座老村舍，其中一座是小酒馆。他们在那儿停了车，一个男人出来，把乏马拴在柱上，给它饮水；老达内尔先生牵起孩子的手，领他沿一条小径穿过田野。

===Original===

The boy could see the country now, but it was all a strange, undiscovered land; they were in the heart of a wilderness of hills and valleys that he had never looked upon, and they were going down a wild, steep hillside, where the narrow path wound in and out amidst gorse and towering bracken, and the sun gleaming out for a moment, there was a gleam of white water far below in a narrow valley, where a little brook poured and rippled from stone to stone. They went down the hill, and through a brake, and then, hidden in dark-green orchards, they came upon a long, low whitewashed house, with a stone roof strangely coloured by the growth of moss and lichens. Mr. Darnell knocked at a heavy oaken door, and they came into a dim room where but little light entered through the thick glass in the deep-set window. There were heavy beams in the ceiling, and a great fireplace sent out an odour of burning wood that Darnell never forgot, and the room seemed to him full of women who talked all together in frightened tones. Mr. Darnell beckoned to a tall, grey old man, who wore corduroy knee-breeches, and the boy, sitting on a high straight-backed chair, could see the old man and his uncle passing to and fro across the windowpanes, as they walked together on the garden path. The women stopped their talk for a moment, and one of them brought him a glass of milk and an apple from some cold inner chamber; and then, suddenly, from a room above there rang out a shrill and terrible shriek, and then, in a young girl’s voice, a more terrible song. It was not like anything the child had ever heard, but as the man recalled it to his memory, he knew to what song it might be compared﻿—to a certain chant indeed that summons the angels and archangels to assist in the great Sacrifice. But as this song chants of the heavenly army, so did that seem to summon all the hierarchy of evil, the hosts of Lilith and Samael; and the words that rang out with such awful modulations﻿—*neumata inferorum*﻿—were in some unknown tongue that few men have ever heard on earth.

The women glared at one another with horror in their eyes, and he saw one or two of the oldest of them clumsily making an old sign upon their breasts. Then they began to speak again, and he remembered fragments of their talk.

“She has been up there,” said one, pointing vaguely over her shoulder.

===Chinese===

孩子这时望得见四野了，可那全然是一块陌生的、未被发现的地方；他们在一大片他从未见过的山岭谷地当中，正走下一道荒峻的山坡，窄径在荆豆和高出人头的蕨丛间绕出绕进；太阳一时破云而出，深深窄谷的底下闪出一带白水，一条小溪在石上翻腾泼溅。他们下了山，穿过一片丛莽，随后，藏在墨绿果园当中，他们望见一长排刷白了的矮房，石头的屋顶让苔藓和地衣长出了奇异的颜色。达内尔先生叩响厚重的橡木门，他们进了一间幽暗的屋子，深凹的窗里厚玻璃透进来的光寥寥。天花板架着粗梁，一座大壁炉发出燃木的气味，达内尔一辈子忘不了；屋子在他眼里坐满了女人，一个个用惊惶的声气同时说话。达内尔先生招手唤来一位高大的灰衣老人，老人穿灯芯绒及膝马裤；孩子坐在一把高背笔直的椅子上，望得见老人同叔父在窗格间来来去去，两人在园径上并肩走。女人们的话头停了一停，其中一个从什么阴凉的内室给他端来一杯牛奶和一只苹果；随后，忽然，楼上一个房间里响起一声尖利可怕的尖叫，接着，一个年轻姑娘的嗓音，唱起一支更可怕的歌。那不像孩子听过的一切；可等这个人把它从记忆里召回，他知道那歌可以比作什么——比作那一支召请众天使、总领天使来助大祭的圣咏。只是那支圣咏唱的是天军，这一支召唤的却像是恶的整个品级，莉莉丝和萨麦尔的军旅；那些字句带着可怕的抑扬迸出来——neumata inferorum（地狱的腔调）——是一种人间的耳朵绝少听过的、未知的语言。

女人们眼里带着惊恐面面相觑，他看见最老的一两个笨拙地在胸前画着古老的记号。随后她们又说起话来，他记得她们谈话的零片。

“她上那儿去过了。”一个说，朝肩后含含糊糊一指。

===Original===

“She’d never know the way,” answered another. “They be all gone that went there.”

“There be nought there in these days.”

“How can you tell that, Gwenllian? ’Tis not for us to say that.”

“My great-grandmother did know some that had been there,” said a very old woman. “She told me how they was taken afterwards.”

And then his uncle appeared at the door, and they went their way as they had come. Edward Darnell never heard any more of it, nor whether the girl died or recovered from her strange attack; but the scene had haunted his mind in boyhood, and now the recollection of it came to him with a certain note of warning, as a symbol of dangers that might be in the way.

---

It would be impossible to carry on the history of Edward Darnell and of Mary his wife to a greater length, since from this point their legend is full of impossible events, and seems to put on the semblance of the stories of the Graal. It is certain, indeed, that in this world they changed their lives, like King Arthur, but this is a work which no chronicler has cared to describe with any amplitude of detail. Darnell, it is true, made a little book, partly consisting of queer verse which might have been written by an inspired infant, and partly made up of “notes and exclamations” in an odd dog-Latin which he had picked up from the “Iolo MSS.,” but it is to be feared that this work, even if published in its entirety, would cast but little light on a perplexing story. He called this piece of literature *In Exitu Israel*, and wrote on the title page the motto, doubtless of his own composition, “*Nunc certe scio quod omnia legenda; omnes historiae, omnes fabulae, omnis Scriptura sint de me narrata.*” It is only too evident that his Latin was not learnt at the feet of Cicero; but in this dialect he relates the great history of the “New Life” as it was manifested to him. The “poems” are even stranger. One, headed (with an odd reminiscence of old-fashioned books) “Lines written on looking down from a Height in London on a Board School suddenly lit up by the Sun” begins thus:﻿—

One day when I was all alone

I found a wondrous little stone,

It lay forgotten on the road

Far from the ways of man’s abode.

When on this stone mine eyes I cast

I saw my Treasure found at last.

I pressed it hard against my face,

I covered it with my embrace,

I hid it in a secret place.

===Chinese===

“她才认不得路呢，”另一个答，“认得路的都死绝了。”

“如今那儿什么也没有了。”

“这话你怎么说得，格温利安？那可不是咱们说得准的。”

“我曾祖母倒认得几个去过的人，”一个很老的女人说，“她跟我说过，他们后来是怎样的下场。”

随后他叔父出现在门口，他们循原路回去了。这件事的下文，爱德华·达内尔再没听说过；那姑娘是死了，还是从那场怪病里缓了过来，也无从知道；可这场景在他少年心里萦回不去；如今回忆起来，带着某一种警告的音信，像征着路上可能有的危险。

---

要把爱德华·达内尔和他妻子玛丽的历史再往下写，是不可能了；因为从这一点起，他们的传说满是不可能的事件，眼看要换上圣杯故事的面目。的确，他们在这个世界里改变了他们的生命，像亚瑟王一样；可这件事，没有哪位编年史家肯费笔墨详叙。达内尔倒是写了一本小书，一半是古怪的诗，出自哪个受了灵感的婴孩之手也不奇怪，一半是“札记与惊叹”，用他从“伊洛文书”里捡来的破烂拉丁文写的；只是恐怕这部著作，哪怕一字不删地印出来，也照不亮这个费解的故事多少。他管这部文献叫 In Exitu Israel（《以色列出埃及记》），扉页上题着一句座右铭，无疑是他自撰的：“Nunc certe scio quod omnia legenda; omnes historiae, omnes fabulae, omnis Scriptura sint de me narrata.”（如今我确知：一切当读之书、一切史、一切寓言、一切经文，说的都是我。）他的拉丁文显然不是在西塞罗脚前学的，一目了然；可他就是用这路方言，讲述那向他显现的“新生命”的大历史。“诗”就更奇了。有一首，题目（带着对旧式书籍的古怪追忆）是《自伦敦高处俯瞰一座突然被日光照亮的寄宿学校有感》，开头是这样：——

有一天，我独自一个
拾得一块奇妙的小石头，
它被遗忘在路上，
远在人烟的居处之外。
我把目光落上这石，
便见我的珍宝终于寻着。
我把它紧紧贴上脸，
我把它拥进怀抱里，
我把它藏进秘密的地方。

===Original===

And every day I went to see

This stone that was my ecstasy;

And worshipped it with flowers rare,

And secret words and sayings fair.

O stone, so rare and red and wise

O fragment of far Paradise,

O Star, whose light is life! O Sea,

Whose ocean is infinity!

Thou art a fire that ever burns,

And all the world to wonder turns;

And all the dust of the dull day

By thee is changed and purged away,

So that, where’er I look, I see

A world of a Great Majesty.

The sullen river rolls all gold,

The desert park’s a faery wold,

When on the trees the wind is borne

I hear the sound of Arthur’s horn

I see no town of grim grey ways,

But a great city all ablaze

With burning torches, to light up

The pinnacles that shrine the Cup.

Ever the magic wine is poured,

Ever the Feast shines on the board,

Ever the song is borne on high

That chants the holy Magistry﻿—

Etc.
							etc.
							etc.

From such documents as these it is clearly impossible to gather any very definite information. But on the last page Darnell has written﻿—

“So I awoke from a dream of a London suburb, of daily labour, of weary, useless little things; and as my eyes were opened I saw that I was in an ancient wood, where a clear well rose into grey film and vapour beneath a misty, glimmering heat. And a form came towards me from the hidden places of the wood, and my love and I were united by the well.”

===Chinese===

我每天都要去看看
这块石头，我的狂喜；
用稀罕的花朵敬拜它，
用秘密的言语和美句。
石头啊，这样稀罕、通红而智慧，
遥远的乐园的碎片啊，
星辰啊，你的光便是生命！海啊，
你的大洋便是无穷！
你是一团永不熄灭的火，
全世界因你化为惊奇；
沉闷日子的全部尘土
都被你点化、被你涤净，
于是无论我望向哪里，
我看见的都是大威严的世界。
阴沉的河流滚着全金，
荒凉的公园成了仙乡，
风吹上树梢的时候，
我听见亚瑟的号角声响；
我望不见惨灰街巷的城，
只望见一座全城燃烧的
大火炬的城市，照亮那
供奉圣杯的塔尖。
魔酒永远倾斟，
盛宴永远照亮几案，
颂歌永远升上高空，
唱着神圣的妙术——
等等。
						等等。
						等等

从这样的文书里，显然采集不出什么确凿的消息。可在末一页上，达内尔写道——

“于是我从一个伦敦郊区的梦里、从每日的劳碌里、从疲倦无用的琐事里醒了过来；睁开眼，我看见自己身在一片古老的树林，一口清澈的井在朦胧灼热的微光里升起灰蒙蒙的水汽。一个身影从树林的隐秘处向我走来；我和我的爱人，在井边结成了一体。”

## The Bowmen / 弓箭手
===Original===

It was during the Retreat of the Eighty Thousand, and the authority of the Censorship is sufficient excuse for not being more explicit. But it was on the most awful day of that awful time, on the day when ruin and disaster came so near that their shadow fell over London far away; and, without any certain news, the hearts of men failed within them and grew faint; as if the agony of the army in the battlefield had entered into their souls.

On this dreadful day, then, when three hundred thousand men in arms with all their artillery swelled like a flood against the little English company, there was one point above all other points in our battle line that was for a time in awful danger, not merely of defeat, but of utter annihilation. With the permission of the Censorship and of the military expert, this corner may, perhaps, be described as a salient, and if this angle were crushed and broken, then the English force as a whole would be shattered, the Allied left would be turned, and Sedan would inevitably follow.

All the morning the German guns had thundered and shrieked against this corner, and against the thousand or so of men who held it. The men joked at the shells, and found funny names for them, and had bets about them, and greeted them with scraps of music-hall songs. But the shells came on and burst, and tore good Englishmen limb from limb, and tore brother from brother, and as the heat of the day increased so did the fury of that terrific cannonade. There was no help, it seemed. The English artillery was good, but there was not nearly enough of it; it was being steadily battered into scrap iron.

There comes a moment in a storm at sea when people say to one another, “It is at its worst; it can blow no harder,” and then there is a blast ten times more fierce than any before it. So it was in these British trenches.

There were no stouter hearts in the whole world than the hearts of these men; but even they were appalled as this seven-times-heated hell of the German cannonade fell upon them and overwhelmed them and destroyed them. And at this very moment they saw from their trenches that a tremendous host was moving against their lines. Five hundred of the thousand remained, and as far as they could see the German infantry was pressing on against them, column upon column, a grey world of men, ten thousand of them, as it appeared afterwards.

===Chinese===

那是在八万人大撤退的日子里；至于不能说得更明白，有书报检查官的权威作充分的借口。但那正是那段可怕岁月里最可怕的一天，那一天，覆灭与灾祸逼近到如此地步，它们的阴影远远地投到了伦敦；没有确实的消息，人心却先自衰竭、发晕，仿佛战场上大军的苦痛渗进了人们的灵魂。

于是，在那个可怕的日子里，当三十万武装到牙齿、带着全部炮队的大军如洪水般压向那一小股英国队伍时，我们战线上有一点，比别处所有各点都更一度陷于可怕的险境——不只是败，而是全军覆没。承书报检查官和军事专家的允许，这个拐角或许可以描述为一个突出部；倘若这个角被砸垮、被击破，那么英军整体便告瓦解，协约军左翼便被迂回，色当的惨局便无可避免。

整个上午，德军的炮火对着这个角，对着守它的千把人，轰鸣、尖啸。士兵们拿炮弹开玩笑，给它们起滑稽的名字，为它们打赌，用杂耍场的歌子的零段迎接它们。可炮弹纷至沓来，爆炸，把好好的英国人撕成碎块，把兄弟从兄弟身边撕走；随着白天暑气的升高，那场骇人炮击的狂怒也步步升高。看来是无救了。英国的炮兵很好，可数量远远不够；正被一寸寸砸成废铁。

海上风暴有一刻，人们彼此说，“顶坏了；再刮不动了”，随后刮来的一阵，比先前任何一阵凶十倍。这几条英军战壕里正是如此。

普天之下，再没有比这些人更硬的心肠了；可当德军炮击那七倍灼热的地狱压下来、淹没他们、摧毁他们时，连他们也胆寒了。也正在这一刻，他们从战壕里望见一支浩浩荡荡的大军正压向他们的防线。千把人剩下五百；极目所至，德军步兵一队接一队向他们压来，一个灰色的人的世界，事后看来，足有一万人。

===Original===

There was no hope at all. They shook hands, some of them. One man improvised a new version of the battlesong, “Goodbye, goodbye to Tipperary,” ending with “And we shan’t get there.” And they all went on firing steadily. The officers pointed out that such an opportunity for high-class, fancy shooting might never occur again; the Germans dropped line after line; the Tipperary humorist asked, “What price Sidney Street?” And the few machine guns did their best. But everybody knew it was of no use. The dead grey bodies lay in companies and battalions, as others came on and on and on, and they swarmed and stirred and advanced from beyond and beyond.

“World without end. Amen,” said one of the British soldiers with some irrelevance as he took aim and fired. And then he remembered﻿—he says he cannot think why or wherefore﻿—a queer vegetarian restaurant in London where he had once or twice eaten eccentric dishes of cutlets made of lentils and nuts that pretended to be steak. On all the plates in this restaurant there was printed a figure of St. George in blue, with the motto, *Adsit Anglis Sanctus Georgius*﻿—May St. George be a present help to the English. This soldier happened to know Latin and other useless things, and now, as he fired at his man in the grey advancing mass﻿—300 yards away﻿—he uttered the pious vegetarian motto. He went on firing to the end, and at last Bill on his right had to clout him cheerfully over the head to make him stop, pointing out as he did so that the King’s ammunition cost money and was not lightly to be wasted in drilling funny patterns into dead Germans.

For as the Latin scholar uttered his invocation he felt something between a shudder and an electric shock pass through his body. The roar of the battle died down in his ears to a gentle murmur; instead of it, he says, he heard a great voice and a shout louder than a thunder-peal crying, “Array, array, array!”

His heart grew hot as a burning coal, it grew cold as ice within him, as it seemed to him that a tumult of voices answered to his summons. He heard, or seemed to hear, thousands shouting: “St. George! St. George!”

“Ha! messire; ha! sweet Saint, grant us good deliverance!”

“St. George for merry England!”

“Harow! Harow! Monseigneur St. George, succour us.”

“Ha! St. George! Ha! St. George! a long bow and a strong bow.”

“Heaven’s Knight, aid us!”

===Chinese===

全无希望了。有些人握了握手。有人即兴把那支战歌改了词，“再见吧，再见吧，蒂珀雷里”，收尾一句改成“咱们是到不了那儿了”。大家照旧沉着地开火。军官们指出，这样高级的、花样射击的机会，怕是再不会有了；德国人一排一排地倒；那位蒂珀雷里的滑稽家问：“西德尼街行情如何？”仅有的几挺机枪也尽了全力。可谁都知道没有用。灰色的尸体成连成营地躺着，后头的还一浪一浪、一浪一浪地涌上来、攒动、推进，从远方又远方。

“世世无尽。阿门。”一个英国士兵瞄着、放着枪，没头没脑地说了这么一句。随后他想起了——他说想不起为什么、凭什么——伦敦一家古怪的素食餐馆，他在那儿吃过一两回用兵豆和坚果冒充牛排的古怪肉饼。那家馆子所有的盘子上都印着一个蓝色的圣乔治像，配着一句铭文，Adsit Anglis Sanctus Georgius——愿圣乔治佑助英格兰人。这位士兵恰巧懂拉丁文，还懂些别的无用之物；此刻，他朝那灰色人潮里他的人放着枪——三百码外——念出了那句虔诚的素食铭文。他一直打到收场，末了他右边的比尔只好开开心心给他脑袋来了一巴掌，叫他住手，一面指出来：国王的弹药是要花钱的，不该拿来在死德国人身上钻好玩的花纹。

因为那位拉丁学者念诵祷词时，他感到一样东西——介于战栗与电击之间——穿身而过。战斗的轰鸣在他耳中沉下去，化作一片轻轻的低语；他说，取而代之的，他听见一个大嗓门，一声比雷还响的呼喊：“列阵，列阵，列阵！”

他的心热得像烧红的炭，又冷得像心里的冰；仿佛千万个声音应着他的召唤掀起一片喧腾。他听见，或像是听见，千万人在喊：“圣乔治！圣乔治！”

“哈！爵爷；哈！亲爱的圣徒，赐我们好解脱！”

“圣乔治保佑快乐的英格兰！”

“哈罗！哈罗！圣乔治大人，救我们。”

“哈！圣乔治！哈！圣乔治！一张长弓，一张强弓。”

“上天的骑士，援助我们！”

===Original===

And as the soldier heard these voices he saw before him, beyond the trench, a long line of shapes, with a shining about them. They were like men who drew the bow, and with another shout their cloud of arrows flew singing and tingling through the air towards the German hosts.

The other men in the trench were firing all the while. They had no hope; but they aimed just as if they had been shooting at Bisley. Suddenly one of them lifted up his voice in the plainest English, “Gawd help us!” he bellowed to the man next to him, “but we’re blooming marvels! Look at those grey﻿ ﻿… gentlemen, look at them! D’ye see them? They’re not going down in dozens, nor in ’undreds; it’s thousands, it is. Look! look! there’s a regiment gone while I’m talking to ye.”

“Shut it!” the other soldier bellowed, taking aim, “what are ye gassing about!”

But he gulped with astonishment even as he spoke, for, indeed, the grey men were falling by the thousands. The English could hear the guttural scream of the German officers, the crackle of their revolvers as they shot the reluctant; and still line after line crashed to the earth.

All the while the Latin-bred soldier heard the cry: “Harow! Harow! Monseigneur, dear saint, quick to our aid! St. George help us!”

“High Chevalier, defend us!”

The singing arrows fled so swift and thick that they darkened the air; the heathen horde melted from before them.

“More machine guns!” Bill yelled to Tom.

“Don’t hear them,” Tom yelled back. “But, thank God, anyway; they’ve got it in the neck.”

In fact, there were ten thousand dead German soldiers left before that salient of the English army, and consequently there was no Sedan. In Germany, a country ruled by scientific principles, the Great General Staff decided that the contemptible English must have employed shells containing an unknown gas of a poisonous nature, as no wounds were discernible on the bodies of the dead German soldiers. But the man who knew what nuts tasted like when they called themselves steak knew also that St. George had brought his Agincourt Bowmen to help the English.

===Chinese===

那士兵听见这些声音时，看见面前、战壕之外，长长一列身影，周身有光华闪耀。他们像一排张弓的人；随着又一声呼喊，他们那阵箭雨唱着、啸着，穿过空气，飞向德军的纵队。

战壕里其余的人一直放着枪。他们没有希望；可他们瞄准的样子，就像在比斯利打靶一般。忽然有一个人扯开顶明白的英语嗓子：“老天爷帮衬！”他朝身边的人吼，“咱们可真是呱呱叫的奇迹！瞧那些灰色的……先生们，瞧啊！瞧见没有？他们不是十个十个倒，也不是一百个一百个倒；是几千几千地倒啊。瞧！瞧！我跟你说话这会儿，又报销了一个团。”

“少废话！”另一个士兵瞄着准吼道，“你咕哝个什么劲！”

可他说话间也惊得直咽口水，因为，真的，灰色的人正成千成千地倒下。英国人听得见德国军官相哑的吼叫，听得见他们左轮枪的脆响——他们正在枪毙不肯往前的人；可队列还是一排接一排轰然扑倒在地。

那受了拉丁文教养的士兵始终听见喊声：“哈罗！哈罗！大人，亲爱的圣徒，快来相助！圣乔治帮我们！”

“高贵的骑士，护卫我们！”

歌唱的箭飞得又急又密，把天都遮暗了；异教的群氓在他们面前消融。

“再要几挺机枪！”比尔冲汤姆喊。

“没听见，”汤姆回喊。“不过，反正感谢上帝；他们挨了报应。”

事实上，英军那个突出部前躺着上万名阵亡的德国士兵，于是，色当没有发生。在按科学原则治理的德国，参谋总部断定：那些可鄙的英国人必定使用了装有某种未知毒气的炮弹，因为阵亡德军士兵的尸体上看不出伤痕。可是，那个尝过坚果冒充牛排滋味的人也知道：圣乔治把他的阿金库尔弓箭手带来了，来帮助英格兰人。

## The Dazzling Light / 炫目的光
===Original===

The new head-covering is made of heavy steel, which has been specialty treated to increase its resisting power. The walls protecting the skull are particularly thick, and the weight of the helmet renders its use in open warfare out of the question. The rim is large, like that of the headpiece of Mambrino, and the soldier can at will either bring the helmet forward and protect his eyes or wear it so as to protect the base of the skull﻿ ﻿… Military experts admit that continuance of the present trench warfare may lead to those engaged in it, especially bombing parties and barbed wire cutters, being more heavily armoured than the knights, who fought at Bouvines and at Agincourt.

> The *Times*, July 22, 1915

The war is already a fruitful mother of legends. Some people think that there are too many war legends, and a Croydon gentleman﻿—or lady, I am not sure which﻿—wrote to me quite recently telling me that a certain particular legend, which I will not specify, had become the “chief horror of the war.” There may be something to be said for this point of view, but it strikes me as interesting that the old myth-making faculty has survived into these days, a relic of noble, far-off Homeric battles. And after all, what do we know? It does not do to be too sure that this, that, or the other hasn’t happened and couldn’t have happened.

What follows, at any rate, has no claim to be considered either as legend or as myth. It is merely one of the odd circumstances of these times, and I have no doubt it can easily be “explained away.” In fact, the rationalistic explanation of the whole thing is patent and on the surface. There is only one little difficulty, and that, I fancy, is by no means insuperable. In any case this one knot or tangle may be put down as a queer coincidence and nothing more.

===Chinese===

新型头盔以厚重的钢材制成，钢材经特殊处理以增强抗力。护颅的壁格外厚，头盔的重量使它在运动战中无法使用。帽檐宽大，如曼布里诺的头盔一般；士兵可随意将盔前倾护住双眼，也可后戴护住后脑……军事专家们承认，倘若眼下的战壕战继续下去，参战人员——尤其是投弹组和剪铁丝网组——身上的甲胄，可能要比布汶和阿金库尔战场上的骑士们更为沉重。

> 《泰晤士报》，1915年7月22日

战争已经是传说的一位多产的母亲。有人觉得战争传说太多了；克罗伊登一位先生——或太太，我说不准是哪位——前不久还写信告诉我，某个我不便指明的传说，已经成了“战争的头号恐怖”。这种观点或许不无道理；但我觉得有意思的是，那古老的造神话的官能竟活到了今日，成了遥远的、高贵的荷马战场的一件遗物。再说，我们又知道什么呢？断言这个、那个没有发生、不可能发生，未免太笃定了。

下面这件事，无论如何，没有资格被当作传说或神话。它只是这些时日里的一桩怪事；我毫不怀疑，它很容易被“解释过去”。事实上，对整件事的理性主义解释是明摆着的，就在表面。只有一个小小的难处；而那个难处，我想，也绝非不可逾越。总之，这一个疙瘩，尽可以记作一次奇怪的巧合，仅此而已。

===Original===

Here, then, is the curiosity or oddity in question. A young fellow, whom we will call for avoidance of all identification Delamere Smith﻿—he is now Lieutenant Delamere Smith﻿—was spending his holidays on the coast of west South Wales at the beginning of the war. He was something or other not very important in the City, and in his leisure hours he smattered lightly and agreeably a little literature, a little art, a little antiquarianism. He liked the Italian primitives, he knew the difference between first, second, and third pointed, he had looked through Boutell’s *Engraved Brasses*. He had been heard indeed to speak with enthusiasm of the brasses of Sir Robert de Septvans and Sir Roger de Trumpington.

One morning﻿—he thinks it must have been the morning of August 16, 1914﻿—the sun shone so brightly into his room that he woke early, and the fancy took him that it would be fine to sit on the cliffs in the pure sunlight. So he dressed and went out, and climbed up Giltar Point, and sat there enjoying the sweet air and the radiance of the sea, and the sight of the fringe of creaming foam about the grey foundations of St. Margaret’s Island. Then he looked beyond and gazed at the new white monastery on Caldy, and wondered who the architect was, and how he had contrived to make the group of buildings look exactly like the background of a medieval picture.

After about an hour of this and a couple of pipes, Smith confesses that he began to feel extremely drowsy. He was just wondering whether it would be pleasant to stretch himself out on the wild thyme that scented the high place and go to sleep till breakfast, when the mounting sun caught one of the monastery windows, and Smith stared sleepily at the darting flashing light till it dazzled him. Then he felt “queer.” There was an odd sensation as if the top of his head were dilating and contracting, and then he says he had a sort of shock, something between a mild current of electricity and the sensation of putting one’s hand into the ripple of a swift brook.

Now, what happened next Smith cannot describe at all clearly. He knew he was on Giltar, looking across the waves to Caldy; he heard all the while the hollow, booming tide in the caverns of the rocks far below him. And yet he saw, as if in a glass, a very different country﻿—a level fenland cut by slow streams, by long avenues of trimmed trees.

===Chinese===

那么，这就是要说的那桩奇事。一个年轻人——为避免对号入座，我们姑且叫他德拉米尔·史密斯——如今是德拉米尔·史密斯中尉——开战之初正在南威尔士西海岸度他的假期。他在城里当个不甚紧要的什么差事；闲暇时，他浅浅地、却颇有雅趣地涉猎一点文学、一点艺术、一点古物学。他喜欢意大利原始派画家，分得清第一、第二、第三尖拱的区别，还翻过鲍特尔的《雕刻黄铜纪念像》。确实有人听他热烈地谈过瑟普特文斯的罗伯特爵士和特朗普顿的罗杰爵士的黄铜像。

一天早晨——他想那准是1914年8月16日的早晨——太阳照进他房里那样亮，他早早醒了，忽发奇想：坐在崖顶上晒纯净的日光该多美。于是他穿衣出门，爬上吉尔塔角，坐在那儿，享受甜美的空气和海上的辉光，看圣玛格丽特岛灰色基座四周镶着的一圈奶油色泡沫。然后他望向更远处，凝望卡尔迪岛上那座崭新的白色修道院，心里琢磨建筑师是谁，他用了什么法子，叫那一群房子看上去活脱脱是一幅中世纪绘画的背景。
