“罪人也是如此，同圣徒一样。倘若你遇见一个非常恶的人，又认出了他的恶；无疑，他会叫你充满恐怖与敬畏；可你并没有理由‘讨厌’他。相反，倘若你能把那罪从心里排除出去，你很可能发现这罪人是极好的伴侣，过不了多久，你恐怕还得对自己讲一番道理，才把恐怖找回来。可它到底是何等可怕啊。倘若明天早晨，玫瑰和百合突然唱起歌来；倘若家具开始列队行进，像莫泊桑那篇小说里写的那样！”

===Original===

“I am glad you have come back to that comparison,” said Cotgrave, “because I wanted to ask you what it is that corresponds in humanity to these imaginary feats of inanimate things. In a word﻿—what is sin? You have given me, I know, an abstract definition, but I should like a concrete example.”

“I told you it was very rare,” said Ambrose, who appeared willing to avoid the giving of a direct answer. “The materialism of the age, which has done a good deal to suppress sanctity, has done perhaps more to suppress evil. We find the earth so very comfortable that we have no inclination either for ascents or descents. It would seem as if the scholar who decided to ‘specialize’ in Tophet, would be reduced to purely antiquarian researches. No palæontologist could show you a *live* pterodactyl.”

“And yet you, I think, have ‘specialized,’ and I believe that your researches have descended to our modern times.”

“You are really interested, I see. Well, I confess, that I have dabbled a little, and if you like I can show you something that bears on the very curious subject we have been discussing.”

Ambrose took a candle and went away to a far, dim corner of the room. Cotgrave saw him open a venerable bureau that stood there, and from some secret recess he drew out a parcel, and came back to the window where they had been sitting.

Ambrose undid a wrapping of paper, and produced a green pocketbook.

“You will take care of it?” he said. “Don’t leave it lying about. It is one of the choicer pieces in my collection, and I should be very sorry if it were lost.”

He fondled the faded binding.

“I knew the girl who wrote this,” he said. “When you read it, you will see how it illustrates the talk we have had tonight. There is a sequel, too, but I won’t talk of that.”

===Chinese===

“很高兴你又回到那个比方，”科特格雷夫说，“因为我正想问你：人心人事里，同这些无生命之物的假想行径相对应的，究竟是什么？一句话——罪是什么？我知道你已给了我一个抽象的定义，可我想要一个具体的例子。”

“我告诉过你，它非常稀少，”安布罗斯说，他显然想回避正面的回答。“这个时代的物质主义，压制圣洁出了大力，压制恶出的力恐怕更大。我们觉得这地上太舒服了，上升下降的兴致都没有了。看来，哪位学者若决定‘专攻’托斐特，只好去做纯古董式的研究。没有哪位古生物学家能给你看一只活的翼指龙。”

“可你，我想，已经‘专了攻’，而且我相信，你的研究一直下探到了我们这个时代。”

“我看你是真有兴趣。好吧，我承认，我稍稍涉猎过一点；你若愿意，我可以给你看一样东西，同我们今晚讨论的这个非常古怪的题目正有关涉。”

安布罗斯端起一支蜡烛，走到房间远处一个幽暗的角落。科特格雷夫看见他打开立在那里的一张古旧的写字台，从某个暗屉里摸出一个包裹，回到他们方才坐着的窗前。

安布罗斯解开纸包，取出一本绿色的袖珍簿。

“你会好好保管吧？”他说。“别随手乱放。这是我的藏品里顶精的一件，要是丢了，我会非常心疼。”

他抚着那褪了色的封皮。

“写这本子的那个姑娘，我认识，”他说。“你读了就会明白，它怎样印证了我们今晚的谈话。这后面还有一段下文，可我不愿谈它。”

===Original===

“There was an odd article in one of the reviews some months ago,” he began again, with the air of a man who changes the subject. “It was written by a doctor﻿—Dr. Coryn, I think, was the name. He says that a lady, watching her little girl playing at the drawing-room window, suddenly saw the heavy sash give way and fall on the child’s fingers. The lady fainted, I think, but at any rate the doctor was summoned, and when he had dressed the child’s wounded and maimed fingers he was summoned to the mother. She was groaning with pain, and it was found that three fingers of her hand, corresponding with those that had been injured on the child’s hand, were swollen and inflamed, and later, in the doctor’s language, purulent sloughing set in.”

Ambrose still handled delicately the green volume.

“Well, here it is,” he said at last, parting with difficulty, it seemed, from his treasure.

“You will bring it back as soon as you have read it,” he said, as they went out into the hall, into the old garden, faint with the odour of white lilies.

There was a broad red band in the east as Cotgrave turned to go, and from the high ground where he stood he saw that awful spectacle of London in a dream.

===Chinese===

“几个月前，有本评论刊物上登过一篇奇怪的文章，”他又开口了，一副换话题的神气。“作者是位医生——我想名字是科林医生。他说，有位太太看着她的小女儿在客厅窗前玩，忽然看见沉重的窗框脱了轨，砸在孩子手指上。那位太太晕了过去——我想是这样；反正医生请来了，等他给孩子受伤残缺的手指包扎好，又被请去看母亲。她正痛得呻吟，人们发现，她手上同孩子受伤手指相对应的三根手指，肿了，发炎了，随后——用医生的话说——出现了化脓性溃烂。”

安布罗斯仍旧温柔地摩挲着那本绿簿子。

“好吧，就是这个了，”他终于说，同他的珍藏分手，像是分得很艰难。

“读完立刻送回来，”他说；这时他们走进门厅，走进那飘着淡淡白百合香气的旧园子。

科特格雷夫转身要走时，东方横着一道宽阔的红光；从他站的高地上，他望见了伦敦——那如在梦中的骇人景象。

### The Green Book / 绿皮书

### The Green Book /

### The Green Book /

### The Green Book / 
===Original===

The morocco binding of the book was faded, and the colour had grown faint, but there were no stains nor bruises nor marks of usage. The book looked as if it had been bought “on a visit to London” some seventy or eighty years ago, and had somehow been forgotten and suffered to lie away out of sight. There was an old, delicate, lingering odour about it, such an odour as sometimes haunts an ancient piece of furniture for a century or more. The end-papers, inside the binding, were oddly decorated with coloured patterns and faded gold. It looked small, but the paper was fine, and there were many leaves, closely covered with minute, painfully formed characters.

===Chinese===

这本子的摩洛哥皮封面褪了色，颜色淡了下去，却没有污渍，没有磕碰，没有翻用的痕迹。它像是七八十年前“来伦敦游玩”时买的，不知怎么就被忘掉，搁在了一处看不见的地方。它带着一股陈年的、幽微的、缠绵不去的气味，像有时一件古老公寓家具上一百年也不散的那种气味。封面里面的环衬，古怪地饰着彩色的花纹和褪色的金。它看着小，纸却细，页数很多，密密麻麻写满了极小的、一笔一画都吃力的字。

===Original===

I found this book (the manuscript began) in a drawer in the old bureau that stands on the landing. It was a very rainy day and I could not go out, so in the afternoon I got a candle and rummaged in the bureau. Nearly all the drawers were full of old dresses, but one of the small ones looked empty, and I found this book hidden right at the back. I wanted a book like this, so I took it to write in. It is full of secrets. I have a great many other books of secrets I have written, hidden in a safe place, and I am going to write here many of the old secrets and some new ones; but there are some I shall not put down at all. I must not write down the real names of the days and months which I found out a year ago, nor the way to make the Aklo letters, or the Chian language, or the great beautiful Circles, nor the Mao Games, nor the chief songs. I may write something about all these things but not the way to do them, for peculiar reasons. And I must not say who the Nymphs are, or the Dôls, or Jeelo, or what *voolas* mean. All these are most secret secrets, and I am glad when I remember what they are, and how many wonderful languages I know, but there are some things that I call the secrets of the secrets of the secrets that I dare not think of unless I am quite alone, and then I shut my eyes, and put my hands over them and whisper the word, and the Alala comes. I only do this at night in my room or in certain woods that I know, but I must not describe them, as they are secret woods. Then there are the Ceremonies, which are all of them important, but some are more delightful than others﻿—there are the White Ceremonies, and the Green Ceremonies, and the Scarlet Ceremonies. The Scarlet Ceremonies are the best, but there is only one place where they can be performed properly, though there is a very nice imitation which I have done in other places. Besides these, I have the dances, and the Comedy, and I have done the Comedy sometimes when the others were looking, and they didn’t understand anything about it. I was very little when I first knew about these things.

===Chinese===

我找到这本簿子（手稿是这样开头的）是在楼梯口那张旧写字台的一个抽屉里。那天下大雨，我出不去，下午便点了支蜡烛去翻写字台。差不多所有的抽屉都塞满旧衣裳，只有一个小抽屉看着是空的，我发现这本簿子就藏在最里头。我正想要一本这样的簿子，就拿来写了。它里面全是秘密。我还写过许多别的秘密簿，藏在安全的地方；我要在这里写下许多旧秘密，也写一些新的；可有些秘密我根本不会写下来。一年前我发现了日子和月份的真名字，还有阿克洛字母（Aklo letters）的做法、奇安语（Chian）、又大又美的圆环、毛游戏（Mao Games）、还有主要的歌——这些我都不能写。这些事我都可以写一点，但不能写做法，因为有特别的缘故。还有宁芙们是谁、多尔斯（Dôls）是谁、吉洛（Jeelo）是什么、沃拉斯（voolas）是什么意思，我也都不能说。全都是最秘密的秘密；我一想起它们是什么、想起我懂多少种奇妙的语言，就很欢喜。可有些东西，我叫它们秘密中秘密中的秘密，不是独自一人的时候，我连想都不敢想；那时我就闭上眼睛，用手捂住它们，低声说出那个词，阿啦啦（Alala）就来了。这种事我只在夜里自己房间里做，或在几处我认识的林子里做；可那些林子我不能描写，因为是秘密的林子。还有各种仪典，全都重要，有些却比别的更叫人欢喜——有白色仪典，有绿色仪典，有猩红仪典。猩红仪典最好，可只有一个地方能做得地道，不过在别处我做过一种很好的模仿。此外我还有各种舞，还有那出喜剧；有时别人看着，我也演过喜剧，他们一点儿也不懂。我头一回知道这些事的时候，还很小很小。

===Original===

When I was very small, and mother was alive, I can remember remembering things before that, only it has all got confused. But I remember when I was five or six I heard them talking about me when they thought I was not noticing. They were saying how queer I was a year or two before, and how nurse had called my mother to come and listen to me talking all to myself, and I was saying words that nobody could understand. I was speaking the Xu language, but I only remember a very few of the words, as it was about the little white faces that used to look at me when I was lying in my cradle. They used to talk to me, and I learnt their language and talked to them in it about some great white place where they lived, where the trees and the grass were all white, and there were white hills as high up as the moon, and a cold wind. I have often dreamed of it afterwards, but the faces went away when I was very little. But a wonderful thing happened when I was about five. My nurse was carrying me on her shoulder; there was a field of yellow corn, and we went through it, it was very hot. Then we came to a path through a wood, and a tall man came after us, and went with us till we came to a place where there was a deep pool, and it was very dark and shady. Nurse put me down on the soft moss under a tree, and she said: “She can’t get to the pond now.” So they left me there, and I sat quite still and watched, and out of the water and out of the wood came two wonderful white people, and they began to play and dance and sing. They were a kind of creamy white like the old ivory figure in the drawing-room; one was a beautiful lady with kind dark eyes, and a grave face, and long black hair, and she smiled such a strange sad smile at the other, who laughed and came to her. They played together, and danced round and round the pool, and they sang a song till I fell asleep. Nurse woke me up when she came back, and she was looking something like the lady had looked, so I told her all about it, and asked her why she looked like that. At first she cried, and then she looked very frightened, and turned quite pale. She put me down on the grass and stared at me, and I could see she was shaking all over. Then she said I had been dreaming, but I knew I hadn’t. Then she made me promise not to say a word about it to anybody, and if I did I should be thrown into the black pit.

===Chinese===

我很小的时候，妈妈还在世，我能记得在那之前记得的事，只是全都搅混了。可我记得五六岁时，有一回他们当我没在意，说起我来。他们说一两年前我多么古怪，保姆怎样叫妈妈来听我一个人自言自语，说的字谁也听不懂。我说的是徐语（Xu），可那些字我只记得很少几个了，因为它讲的是那些小白脸儿——我躺在摇篮里时，它们常来望我。它们从前同我说话，我学会了它们的语言，用那语言同它们谈它们住的那个大白的地方：那里的树和草全是白的，有白山，高到月亮上，还有冷风。后来我常梦见它，可那些脸儿在我很小的时候就走了。可是在我五岁上下，发生了一件奇妙的事。保姆把我扛在肩上；有一片黄麦子地，我们从中间穿过去，天很热。随后我们走进林中一条小路，一个高个子的男人跟上来，同我们一路走，走到一个有深水潭的地方，那里又暗又荫凉。保姆把我放在树下的软苔藓上，说：“这下她爬不到潭边了。”他们把我撂在那儿，我一动不动地坐着看；从水里、从林子里，出来两个奇妙的白色人，他们开始玩耍、跳舞、唱歌。他们是一种奶油似的白，像客厅里那座老象牙雕像；一位是美丽的太太，有和气的黑眼睛，端庄的脸，长长的黑头发；她朝另一位微笑，那笑那么奇怪、那么忧伤，那一位笑着朝她走去。他们一起玩，围着水潭转圈跳舞，唱了一支歌，唱到我睡着。保姆回来把我弄醒，她那神情有几分像那位太太，我便把这事全告诉了她，问她为什么那副样子。起先她哭了，随后她显得很害怕，脸都白了。她把我放到草地上，直勾勾盯着我，我看得见她浑身发抖。随后她说我是在做梦，可我知道不是。随后她叫我起誓，这事一个字也不对任何人讲；要是讲了，就要被扔进黑坑里去。

===Original===

I was not frightened at all, though nurse was, and I never forgot about it, because when I shut my eyes and it was quite quiet, and I was all alone, I could see them again, very faint and far away, but very splendid; and little bits of the song they sang came into my head, but I couldn’t sing it.

===Chinese===

我一点也不怕，虽然保姆怕；这事我从来没有忘掉，因为每当我闭上眼睛、四下很静、独自一人的时候，我就能再看见他们，很淡、很远，却非常华美；他们唱的那支歌的小小片段会进到我脑袋里来，可我唱不出来。

===Original===

I was thirteen, nearly fourteen, when I had a very singular adventure, so strange that the day on which it happened is always called the White Day. My mother had been dead for more than a year, and in the morning I had lessons, but they let me go out for walks in the afternoon. And this afternoon I walked a new way, and a little brook led me into a new country, but I tore my frock getting through some of the difficult places, as the way was through many bushes, and beneath the low branches of trees, and up thorny thickets on the hills, and by dark woods full of creeping thorns. And it was a long, long way. It seemed as if I was going on for ever and ever, and I had to creep by a place like a tunnel where a brook must have been, but all the water had dried up, and the floor was rocky, and the bushes had grown overhead till they met, so that it was quite dark. And I went on and on through that dark place; it was a long, long way. And I came to a hill that I never saw before. I was in a dismal thicket full of black twisted boughs that tore me as I went through them, and I cried out because I was smarting all over, and then I found that I was climbing, and I went up and up a long way, till at last the thicket stopped and I came out crying just under the top of a big bare place, where there were ugly grey stones lying all about on the grass, and here and there a little twisted, stunted tree came out from under a stone, like a snake. And I went up, right to the top, a long way. I never saw such big ugly stones before; they came out of the earth some of them, and some looked as if they had been rolled to where they were, and they went on and on as far as I could see, a long, long way. I looked out from them and saw the country, but it was strange. It was winter time, and there were black terrible woods hanging from the hills all round; it was like seeing a large room hung with black curtains, and the shape of the trees seemed quite different from any I had ever seen before. I was afraid. Then beyond the woods there were other hills round in a great ring, but I had never seen any of them; it all looked black, and everything had a voor over it. It was all so still and silent, and the sky was heavy and grey and sad, like a wicked voorish dome in Deep Dendo. I went on into the dreadful rocks. There were hundreds and hundreds of them.

===Chinese===

我十三岁、快十四岁的时候，遭了一场非常离奇的奇遇，离奇得那么厉害，出事的那一天后来一直被叫作白日。我妈妈已经死了一年多，上午我有功课，下午他们许我出去散步。那天下午我走了一条新路，一条小溪把我引进一片新的地方；可穿过几处难走的地方时我把裙子撕破了，因为一路尽是树丛，要从低垂的树枝底下过，要爬山上多刺的灌木丛，还要沿着一座座黑树林走，林里满是爬蔓的荆棘。那是好长好长的路。仿佛我一直走啊走，永远走不到头；我还得爬过一段像隧道的地方，那里从前准是一条溪，水全干了，底下是石头，上头的灌木长到合了拢，里头黑透了。我在那黑地方走啊走；好长好长的路。我到了一座从前从没见过的小山。我在一片凄惨的灌木丛里，满是一根根拧黑了的枝子，穿过去时把我刮得生疼，我哭喊起来，因为浑身火辣辣的；随后我发觉自己在往上爬，爬了好长一段，末了灌木丛到头了，我哭着钻出来，正好在一大片光秃秃的高地的顶底下，那里丑陋的灰石头东一块西一块躺在草上，偶尔有一棵拧巴的、长不大的小树从石头底下钻出来，像一条蛇。我往上走，一直走到顶，好长的路。我从前从没见过那样又大又丑的石头；有的从地里长出来，有的像是被滚到了它待的地方，一直铺下去，望到多远是多远，好长好长的路。我从石头中间望出去，看见了四下的乡野，可它怪得很。那时是冬天，一圈山崖上垂挂着黑黢黢的可怕树林；就像看一间挂满黑帘子的大屋子，树的形状同我从前见过的全都不同。我害怕了。树林再往外，还有别的山，围成大大的一圈，可那些山我一座也没见过；一切看着都是黑的，样样东西都罩着一层沃尔（voor）。四下那么静、那么无声，天又重又灰又愁，像深登多（Deep Dendo）里一顶邪恶的、沃尔似的穹顶。我往那些可怕的石头里走去。它们有成百上千块。

===Original===

Some were like horrid-grinning men; I could see their faces as if they would jump at me out of the stone, and catch hold of me, and drag me with them back into the rock, so that I should always be there. And there were other rocks that were like animals, creeping, horrible animals, putting out their tongues, and others were like words that I could not say, and others like dead people lying on the grass. I went on among them, though they frightened me, and my heart was full of wicked songs that they put into it; and I wanted to make faces and twist myself about in the way they did, and I went on and on a long way till at last I liked the rocks, and they didn’t frighten me any more. I sang the songs I thought of; songs full of words that must not be spoken or written down. Then I made faces like the faces on the rocks, and I twisted myself about like the twisted ones, and I lay down flat on the ground like the dead ones, and I went up to one that was grinning, and put my arms round him and hugged him. And so I went on and on through the rocks till I came to a round mound in the middle of them. It was higher than a mound, it was nearly as high as our house, and it was like a great basin turned upside down, all smooth and round and green, with one stone, like a post, sticking up at the top. I climbed up the sides, but they were so steep I had to stop or I should have rolled all the way down again, and I should have knocked against the stones at the bottom, and perhaps been killed. But I wanted to get up to the very top of the big round mound, so I lay down flat on my face, and took hold of the grass with my hands and drew myself up, bit by bit, till I was at the top. Then I sat down on the stone in the middle, and looked all round about. I felt I had come such a long, long way, just as if I were a hundred miles from home, or in some other country, or in one of the strange places I had read about in the *Tales of the Genie* and the *Arabian Nights* or as if I had gone across the sea, far away, for years and I had found another world that nobody had ever seen or heard of before, or as if I had somehow flown through the sky and fallen on one of the stars I had read about where everything is dead and cold and grey, and there is no air, and the wind doesn’t blow. I sat on the stone and looked all round and down and round about me.

===Chinese===

有的像狞笑的吓人的男人；我看得见他们的脸，仿佛就要从石头里朝我跳出来，抓住我，把我同他们一起拖回石头里去，叫我在那里待一辈子。还有的石头像野兽，爬行的、可怕的野兽，吐着舌头；有的像我说不出口的字；有的像躺在草上的死人。我在它们中间往前走，虽然它们吓我，我的心被它们塞满了邪恶的歌；我想做鬼脸，想学它们的样子把身子拧过来拧过去；我走啊走，走了好长，末了我竟然喜欢上那些石头，它们不再吓我了。我唱起想起来的那些歌；歌里满是决不许说出口、也决不许写下来的字。随后我学石头上那些脸做鬼脸，学拧巴的那些把自己拧来拧去，学死掉的那些直挺挺躺在地上；我走到一块正狞笑着的跟前，张开胳膊抱住它，搂了搂它。就这样我在石头中间走啊走，走到它们正当中的一座圆土丘跟前。它比土丘高，差不多有我们家房子那么高，像一只倒扣的大盆，通体又滑又圆又绿，顶上竖着一块石头，像根柱子。我往上爬，坡太陡了，只好停下，不然会一路滚下去，撞在底下的石头上，说不定摔死。可我想到那座大圆丘的顶上去，就趴下来，脸贴着地，两手抓住草，一点一点把自己拽上去，到了顶上。随后我坐在当中那块石头上，朝四下望。我觉得自己走了好长好长的路，仿佛离家一百英里，仿佛到了别的国土，到了我在《精灵的故事》和《天方夜谭》里读到过的那种怪地方；又仿佛漂洋过海、走了好多年，找到了一个谁也没见过、谁也没听说过的世界；又仿佛不知怎么飞过了天，落在我读到过的一颗星星上，那里样样东西都死了、冷了、灰了，没有空气，风也不吹。我坐在石头上，朝四面望，朝下望，朝我周围望。

===Original===

It was just as if I was sitting on a tower in the middle of a great empty town, because I could see nothing all around but the grey rocks on the ground. I couldn’t make out their shapes any more, but I could see them on and on for a long way, and I looked at them, and they seemed as if they had been arranged into patterns, and shapes, and figures. I knew they couldn’t be, because I had seen a lot of them coming right out of the earth, joined to the deep rocks below, so I looked again, but still I saw nothing but circles, and small circles inside big ones, and pyramids, and domes, and spires, and they seemed all to go round and round the place where I was sitting, and the more I looked, the more I saw great big rings of rocks, getting bigger and bigger, and I stared so long that it felt as if they were all moving and turning, like a great wheel, and I was turning, too, in the middle. I got quite dizzy and queer in the head, and everything began to be hazy and not clear, and I saw little sparks of blue light, and the stones looked as if they were springing and dancing and twisting as they went round and round and round. I was frightened again, and I cried out loud, and jumped up from the stone I was sitting on, and fell down. When I got up I was so glad they all looked still, and I sat down on the top and slid down the mound, and went on again. I danced as I went in the peculiar way the rocks had danced when I got giddy, and I was so glad I could do it quite well, and I danced and danced along, and sang extraordinary songs that came into my head. At last I came to the edge of that great flat hill, and there were no more rocks, and the way went again through a dark thicket in a hollow. It was just as bad as the other one I went through climbing up, but I didn’t mind this one, because I was so glad I had seen those singular dances and could imitate them. I went down, creeping through the bushes, and a tall nettle stung me on my leg, and made me burn, but I didn’t mind it, and I tingled with the boughs and the thorns, but I only laughed and sang. Then I got out of the thicket into a close valley, a little secret place like a dark passage that nobody ever knows of, because it was so narrow and deep and the woods were so thick round it.

===Chinese===

那正像我坐在一座大空城中央的一座塔上，因为四下里除了地上的灰石头，我什么也看不见。它们的形状我认不出来了，可我看得见它们一直铺出去好远；我望着它们，它们像是被排成了图案、形状、形象。我知道不可能，因为我看见许多石头是直接从地里长出来的，同底下深处的大石连着；于是我再仔细看，可看见的仍旧只有圆圈，大圈套小圈，还有角锥、圆顶、尖塔，它们仿佛都围着我坐的地方转啊转；我越看，看见的石头大环越多，一圈比一圈大；我盯得太久，觉得它们全都在动、在转，像一只大轮子，而我就在当中跟着转。我头晕起来，脑袋里怪怪的，样样东西开始发虚、不清爽，我看见一点点蓝色的火星，石头们仿佛在蹦、在跳、在拧，一圈又一圈地转。我又害怕了，大声喊出来，从坐着的石头上跳起来，摔倒了。等我爬起来，看见它们全都安安静静，我高兴极了；我在丘顶坐下，从丘上滑下去，接着往前走。我一路跳着舞，用的就是刚才我头晕时石头们跳的那种怪舞步；我能跳得很像，高兴极了，一路跳啊跳，唱着钻进脑袋里的种种怪歌。末了我走到那片大平山的边上，石头没有了，路又钻进洼地里一片黑灌木丛。它同我爬上来时穿过的那片一样难走，可我全不在乎，因为我太欢喜了——我看见了那些奇异的舞蹈，还能学它们。我往下走，在树丛里爬行，一棵高荨麻螫了我的腿，火烧火燎，我也不管；枝子和刺扎得我又麻又痛，我只管笑，只管唱。随后我钻出灌木丛，进了一条窄谷，一个小小的秘密地方，像一条黑黑的过道，谁也不会知道，因为它那么窄、那么深，四周的林子又那么密。

===Original===

There is a steep bank with trees hanging over it, and there the ferns keep green all through the winter, when they are dead and brown upon the hill, and the ferns there have a sweet, rich smell like what oozes out of fir trees. There was a little stream of water running down this valley, so small that I could easily step across it. I drank the water with my hand, and it tasted like bright, yellow wine, and it sparkled and bubbled as it ran down over beautiful red and yellow and green stones, so that it seemed alive and all colours at once. I drank it, and I drank more with my hand, but I couldn’t drink enough, so I lay down and bent my head and sucked the water up with my lips. It tasted much better, drinking it that way, and a ripple would come up to my mouth and give me a kiss, and I laughed, and drank again, and pretended there was a nymph, like the one in the old picture at home, who lived in the water and was kissing me. So I bent low down to the water, and put my lips softly to it, and whispered to the nymph that I would come again. I felt sure it could not be common water, I was so glad when I got up and went on; and I danced again and went up and up the valley, under hanging hills. And when I came to the top, the ground rose up in front of me, tall and steep as a wall, and there was nothing but the green wall and the sky. I thought of “forever and forever, world without end, Amen”; and I thought I must have really found the end of the world, because it was like the end of everything, as if there could be nothing at all beyond, except the kingdom of Voor, where the light goes when it is put out, and the water goes when the sun takes it away. I began to think of all the long, long way I had journeyed, how I had found a brook and followed it, and followed it on, and gone through bushes and thorny thickets, and dark woods full of creeping thorns. Then I had crept up a tunnel under trees, and climbed a thicket, and seen all the grey rocks, and sat in the middle of them when they turned round, and then I had gone on through the grey rocks and come down the hill through the stinging thicket and up the dark valley, all a long, long way. I wondered how I should get home again, if I could ever find the way, and if my home was there any more, or if it were turned and everybody in it into grey rocks, as in the *Arabian Nights*.

===Chinese===

谷边有一道陡坡，树上垂下枝子来；那里的蕨草一冬都绿着——山上的蕨草早就枯黄了——谷里的蕨草有一种甜润浓艳的香气，像枞树里渗出来的东西。谷底流着一线小溪，小得我一抬脚就能跨过去。我用手捧水喝，那水尝着像明亮的黄酒，从红的、黄的、绿的美丽石头上淌下来，一路起泡闪光，像是活的，什么颜色一时都有。我喝了，又用手捧着喝，怎么也喝不够，就趴下去，低下头，用嘴唇把水吸上来。那样喝着，味道好得多；一圈涟漪会荡到我嘴边，亲我一亲；我笑了，再喝，假装水里住着一位宁芙，像家里那幅古画上的，正亲我。于是我朝水面低低俯下去，把嘴唇轻轻贴上去，对宁芙低声说，我还要再来。我笃定这不是平常的水；站起来接着走时，心里那么欢喜；我又跳起舞，沿着窄谷往上、往上走，头顶是悬垂的山。走到头，地面在我面前高高竖起，像一堵墙又高又陡，除了绿墙和天，什么也没有。我想起“永永远远，世世无穷，阿门”；我想我一定是真的找到了世界的尽头，因为它像是一切的尽头，仿佛墙那边根本不可能再有什么，只除了沃尔国——灯灭了，光就去那里；太阳把水收走了，水就去那里。我开始回想我走了的好长好长的路：怎样遇见一条小溪就跟着它，一直跟；怎样钻过树丛和多刺的荆棘，走过满是爬蔓荆棘的黑树林；怎样从树底下爬过一条隧道，攀上灌木坡，看见了满山的灰石头，还在它们转圈时坐在它们当中；随后又怎样穿过灰石头，从螫人的灌木丛下山，走上这条黑谷——全是好长好长的路。我不知道自己还能不能找到路回家，家里还在不在；还是家已经变成了灰石头，家里的人也全都变成了灰石头，像《天方夜谭》里那样。

===Original===

So I sat down on the grass and thought what I should do next. I was tired, and my feet were hot with walking, and as I looked about I saw there was a wonderful well just under the high, steep wall of grass. All the ground round it was covered with bright, green, dripping moss; there was every kind of moss there, moss like beautiful little ferns, and like palms and fir trees, and it was all green as jewellery, and drops of water hung on it like diamonds. And in the middle was the great well, deep and shining and beautiful, so clear that it looked as if I could touch the red sand at the bottom, but it was far below. I stood by it and looked in, as if I were looking in a glass. At the bottom of the well, in the middle of it, the red grains of sand were moving and stirring all the time, and I saw how the water bubbled up, but at the top it was quite smooth, and full and brimming. It was a great well, large like a bath, and with the shining, glittering green moss about it, it looked like a great white jewel, with green jewels all round. My feet were so hot and tired that I took off my boots and stockings, and let my feet down into the water, and the water was soft and cold, and when I got up I wasn’t tired any more, and I felt I must go on, farther and farther, and see what was on the other side of the wall. I climbed up it very slowly, going sideways all the time, and when I got to the top and looked over, I was in the queerest country I had seen, stranger even than the hill of the grey rocks. It looked as if earth-children had been playing there with their spades, as it was all hills and hollows, and castles and walls made of earth and covered with grass. There were two mounds like big beehives, round and great and solemn, and then hollow basins, and then a steep mounting wall like the ones I saw once by the seaside where the big guns and the soldiers were. I nearly fell into one of the round hollows, it went away from under my feet so suddenly, and I ran fast down the side and stood at the bottom and looked up. It was strange and solemn to look up.

===Chinese===

于是我坐在草上，想下一步做什么。我累了，两只脚走得发烫；四下一望，看见就在那道又高又陡的草墙底下，有一口奇妙的井。井四周的地面全盖着亮亮的、绿莹莹的、滴水的苔藓；什么样的苔藓都有，像美丽小蕨的，像棕榈和枞树的，全都绿得像宝石，水珠挂在上头，像一颗颗钻石。正当中就是那口大井，又深、又亮、又美，清得仿佛一伸手就能碰到井底的红沙，其实还深得很。我站在井边朝里看，像照一面镜子。井底正中，红的沙粒一直在动、在翻，我看得见水是怎样从底下涌上来的，可井面上平平的，满得齐着井口。那是口大井，有洗澡盆那么大；周围镶着闪闪烁烁的绿苔藓，它就像一大颗白宝石，四周围着一圈绿宝石。我的脚又烫又累，就脱了鞋和袜子，把脚放进水里；水又软又凉，等我站起来，一点也不累了；我觉得我必须接着走，越走越远，去看看墙那一边有什么。我爬得很慢，一直横着挪；爬到顶上探头一看，我到了一个顶顶古怪的地方，比灰石头的山还怪。那地方像土孩子们在那儿玩过铲子，全是土丘和土坑，土堆的城堡和墙，盖着草皮。有两座土丘像大蜂巢，圆圆的、大大的、庄严肃穆的；再过去是一层层圆坑；再过去一面陡陡升起的墙，像我有一回在海边见过的那种架着大炮、站着兵的墙。有一只圆坑，脚底下忽然一空，我差点栽进去；我顺着坡快跑下去，站到坑底，朝上望。朝上望，又怪，又庄严。

===Original===

There was nothing but the grey, heavy sky and the sides of the hollow; everything else had gone away, and the hollow was the whole world, and I thought that at night it must be full of ghosts and moving shadows and pale things when the moon shone down to the bottom at the dead of the night, and the wind wailed up above. It was so strange and solemn and lonely, like a hollow temple of dead heathen gods. It reminded me of a tale my nurse had told me when I was quite little; it was the same nurse that took me into the wood where I saw the beautiful white people. And I remembered how nurse had told me the story one winter night, when the wind was beating the trees against the wall, and crying and moaning in the nursery chimney. She said there was, somewhere or other, a hollow pit, just like the one I was standing in, everybody was afraid to go into it or near it, it was such a bad place. But once upon a time there was a poor girl who said she would go into the hollow pit, and everybody tried to stop her, but she would go. And she went down into the pit and came back laughing, and said there was nothing there at all, except green grass and red stones, and white stones and yellow flowers. And soon after people saw she had most beautiful emerald earrings, and they asked how she got them, as she and her mother were quite poor. But she laughed, and said her earrings were not made of emeralds at all, but only of green grass. Then, one day, she wore on her breast the reddest ruby that anyone had ever seen, and it was as big as a hen’s egg, and glowed and sparkled like a hot burning coal of fire. And they asked how she got it, as she and her mother were quite poor. But she laughed, and said it was not a ruby at all, but only a red stone. Then one day she wore round her neck the loveliest necklace that anyone had ever seen, much finer than the queen’s finest, and it was made of great bright diamonds, hundreds of them, and they shone like all the stars on a night in June. So they asked her how she got it, as she and her mother were quite poor. But she laughed, and said they were not diamonds at all, but only white stones.

===Chinese===

天地间只剩灰沉沉的天，和坑的四面；别的一切都走了，这坑就是整个的世界。我想，夜里夜半，月亮照到坑底、风在上头哀号的时候，这里一定满都是鬼魂、移动的影子和惨白的东西。这里那么怪、那么庄严、那么孤零零，像一座异教死神的空庙。它叫我想起保姆在我很小的时候讲过的一个故事；就是领我进林子、让我看见美丽白色人的那个保姆。我记得那是一个冬夜，风把树刮得打在墙上，在育婴室的烟囱里哭、呜咽。她说，不知在什么地方，有一个空坑，同我正站着的这个一模一样，谁也不敢进去，也不敢走近，那是个顶坏的地方。可是从前有个穷姑娘，说她要进那空坑里去；人人都拦她，她偏要去。她下到坑里，笑着回来了，说里面什么也没有，只有青草和红石头、白石头和黄花。不久人们就看见她戴上了一对顶美的翡翠耳环，问她哪儿来的，因为她和母亲穷得很。她笑着说她那耳环根本不是翡翠的，只是青草。后来有一天，她胸口戴了一颗红得人人没见过的红宝石，有鸡蛋那么大，像一块烧红的炭火，又亮又闪。人们问哪儿来的，因为她和母亲穷得很。她笑着，说那根本不是红宝石，只是一块红石头。又有一天，她脖子上挂了一串人人没见过的顶可爱的项链，比王后最好的那条还精美得多，是用几百颗亮晶晶的大钻石串成的，亮得像六月一夜的满天星。人们问哪儿来的，因为她和母亲穷得很。她笑着，说那根本不是钻石，只是白石头。

===Original===

And one day she went to the Court, and she wore on her head a crown of pure angel-gold, so nurse said, and it shone like the sun, and it was much more splendid than the crown the king was wearing himself, and in her ears she wore the emeralds, and the big ruby was the brooch on her breast, and the great diamond necklace was sparkling on her neck. And the king and queen thought she was some great princess from a long way off, and got down from their thrones and went to meet her, but somebody told the king and queen who she was, and that she was quite poor. So the king asked why she wore a gold crown, and how she got it, as she and her mother were so poor. And she laughed, and said it wasn’t a gold crown at all, but only some yellow flowers she had put in her hair. And the king thought it was very strange, and said she should stay at the Court, and they would see what would happen next. And she was so lovely that everybody said that her eyes were greener than the emeralds, that her lips were redder than the ruby, that her skin was whiter than the diamonds, and that her hair was brighter than the golden crown. So the king’s son said he would marry her, and the king said he might. And the bishop married them, and there was a great supper, and afterwards the king’s son went to his wife’s room. But just when he had his hand on the door, he saw a tall, black man, with a dreadful face, standing in front of the door, and a voice said﻿—

Venture not upon your life,

This is mine own wedded wife.

===Chinese===

有一天她进了王宫，头上戴着一顶纯天使金的冠——保姆是这样说的——亮得像太阳，比国王自己戴的那顶还华丽得多；耳朵上是那对翡翠，胸前别针上是那颗大红宝石，脖子上那串大钻石项链闪闪发光。国王和王后只当她是远道来的一位大公主，都从宝座上下来迎她；可是有人告诉国王和王后她是谁，说她穷得很。国王就问她为什么戴金冠，哪儿来的，因为她和母亲那么穷。她笑着，说那根本不是金冠，不过是几朵黄花，是她插在头发上的。国王觉得这事很怪，说她就留在宫里吧，看看接下来会怎样。她长得那么美，人人都说她的眼睛比翡翠还绿，嘴唇比红宝石还红，皮肤比钻石还白，头发比金冠还亮。王子便说要娶她，国王也应允了。主教给他们行了婚礼，摆了盛大的婚宴；随后王子到妻子的房间里去。可他的手刚碰到门，就看见一个高大的黑男人，脸可怕极了，站在门前，一个声音说——

莫拿性命去冒险，
她是我明媒正娶的眷。

===Original===

Then the king’s son fell down on the ground in a fit. And they came and tried to get into the room, but they couldn’t, and they hacked at the door with hatchets, but the wood had turned hard as iron, and at last everybody ran away, they were so frightened at the screaming and laughing and shrieking and crying that came out of the room. But next day they went in, and found there was nothing in the room but thick black smoke, because the black man had come and taken her away. And on the bed there were two knots of faded grass and a red stone, and some white stones, and some faded yellow flowers. I remembered this tale of nurse’s while I was standing at the bottom of the deep hollow; it was so strange and solitary there, and I felt afraid. I could not see any stones or flowers, but I was afraid of bringing them away without knowing, and I thought I would do a charm that came into my head to keep the black man away. So I stood right in the very middle of the hollow, and I made sure that I had none of those things on me, and then I walked round the place, and touched my eyes, and my lips, and my hair in a peculiar manner, and whispered some queer words that nurse taught me to keep bad things away. Then I felt safe and climbed up out of the hollow, and went on through all those mounds and hollows and walls, till I came to the end, which was high above all the rest, and I could see that all the different shapes of the earth were arranged in patterns, something like the grey rocks, only the pattern was different. It was getting late, and the air was indistinct, but it looked from where I was standing something like two great figures of people lying on the grass. And I went on, and at last I found a certain wood, which is too secret to be described, and nobody knows of the passage into it, which I found out in a very curious manner, by seeing some little animal run into the wood through it. So I went after the animal by a very narrow dark way, under thorns and bushes, and it was almost dark when I came to a kind of open place in the middle. And there I saw the most wonderful sight I have ever seen, but it was only for a minute, as I ran away directly, and crept out of the wood by the passage I had come by, and ran and ran as fast as ever I could, because I was afraid, what I had seen was so wonderful and so strange and beautiful.

===Chinese===

王子当场倒在地上，抽了过去。众人赶来想进屋，进不去；他们用斧子劈门，木头却变得同铁一样硬；末了大家都跑了，屋里传出来的尖叫、狂笑、哀号和哭喊，把他们吓破了胆。可第二天他们进了屋，发现屋里什么也没有，只有浓密的黑烟，因为那黑男人来把她带走了。床上搁着两绺枯草、一块红石头、几块白石头、几朵蔫掉的黄花。我站在深坑底时，想起了保姆的这个故事；那里又怪又孤，我心里害怕起来。我看不见什么石头花朵，可我怕自己不知不觉把它们带走，就想做一道灵符，是那时候正好想起来的，好把黑男人挡在外面。于是我站到坑的正当中，先查看清楚自己身上一样那样的东西都没有，然后绕着这地方走了一圈，用一种特别的姿势碰碰眼睛、嘴唇和头发，低声念了几个古怪的字，是保姆教我避邪的。随后我就觉得安全了，爬出坑去，穿过那些土丘、圆坑和土墙，一直走到尽头，那里比哪儿都高，我看见地上各样形状排成了图案，有点像灰石头，只是图案不一样。天晚了，空气朦胧，可从我站的地方看去，那有点像两个巨大的人形躺在草上。我接着走，末了找到一片林子，它太秘密了，不能描写；没有人知道进去的通道，我是非常偶然地发现的——看见一只小兽从那里钻进了林子。我就跟着那小兽走，一条又窄又黑的路，在荆棘和灌木底下；等我走到当中一小块空地时，天差不多黑了。在那里，我看见了我这辈子见过的最奇妙的景象，可只看了一分钟，因为我立刻就跑了，从来路爬出林子，能跑多快跑多快，因为我害怕——我看见的东西，太奇妙、太古怪、太美了。

===Original===

But I wanted to get home and think of it, and I did not know what might not happen if I stayed by the wood. I was hot all over and trembling, and my heart was beating, and strange cries that I could not help came from me as I ran from the wood. I was glad that a great white moon came up from over a round hill and showed me the way, so I went back through the mounds and hollows and down the close valley, and up through the thicket over the place of the grey rocks, and so at last I got home again. My father was busy in his study, and the servants had not told about my not coming home, though they were frightened, and wondered what they ought to do, so I told them I had lost my way, but I did not let them find out the real way I had been. I went to bed and lay awake all through the night, thinking of what I had seen. When I came out of the narrow way, and it looked all shining, though the air was dark, it seemed so certain, and all the way home I was quite sure that I had seen it, and I wanted to be alone in my room, and be glad over it all to myself, and shut my eyes and pretend it was there, and do all the things I would have done if I had not been so afraid. But when I shut my eyes the sight would not come, and I began to think about my adventures all over again, and I remembered how dusky and queer it was at the end, and I was afraid it must be all a mistake, because it seemed impossible it could happen. It seemed like one of nurse’s tales, which I didn’t really believe in, though I was frightened at the bottom of the hollow; and the stories she told me when I was little came back into my head, and I wondered whether it was really there what I thought I had seen, or whether any of her tales could have happened a long time ago. It was so queer; I lay awake there in my room at the back of the house, and the moon was shining on the other side towards the river, so the bright light did not fall upon the wall. And the house was quite still. I had heard my father come upstairs, and just after the clock struck twelve, and after the house was still and empty, as if there was nobody alive in it.

===Chinese===

可我想回家去想它，也不知道再待在林子边上会出什么事。我浑身滚烫，直打哆嗦，心咚咚跳；我从林子边跑开时，喉咙里发出我自己也忍不住的怪叫。幸好一轮又大又白的月亮从一座圆山后头升上来，给我照了路；我便往回走，穿过土丘和圆坑，下了窄谷，从灰石头那地方顶上的灌木丛爬上去，末了终于到了家。父亲在书房里忙，仆人们虽然吓坏了、正商量怎么办，却没有把我没回家的事告诉他；我说我迷了路，可没让他们打听出我真正走的是哪条路。我上了床，一夜没合眼，想着我看见的东西。钻出那条窄路时，天明明黑了，它却看着亮堂堂的；那是那么确凿；一路回家我全拿得稳：我是真的看见了。我想独自待在自己房里，一个人为它欢喜，闭上眼睛假装它还在，把那些要不是害怕就做了的事都做一遍。可我一闭上眼，那景象就不肯来；我又把我的奇遇从头想了一遍，想起末了那里又暗又怪，我怕这全是弄错了，因为这种事像是不可能发生的。它像保姆讲的一个故事，我其实并不真信，虽然在坑底时我害怕了；她小时候讲给我的那些故事又都回到我脑袋里，我纳闷：我自以为看见的东西是不是真在那里，还是她的哪个故事很久以前真发生过。太怪了；我躺在屋子后部的房间里睡不着，月亮照着朝河的那一面，亮光没有落在这面墙上。屋子里静极了。我听见父亲上楼来过；钟刚敲过十二下，随后屋子就又静又空，仿佛里面没有一个活人。

===Original===

And though it was all dark and indistinct in my room, a pale glimmering kind of light shone in through the white blind, and once I got up and looked out, and there was a great black shadow of the house covering the garden, looking like a prison where men are hanged; and then beyond it was all white; and the wood shone white with black gulfs between the trees. It was still and clear, and there were no clouds on the sky. I wanted to think of what I had seen but I couldn’t, and I began to think of all the tales that nurse had told me so long ago that I thought I had forgotten, but they all came back, and mixed up with the thickets and the grey rocks and the hollows in the earth and the secret wood, till I hardly knew what was new and what was old, or whether it was not all dreaming. And then I remembered that hot summer afternoon, so long ago, when nurse left me by myself in the shade, and the white people came out of the water and out of the wood, and played, and danced, and sang, and I began to fancy that nurse told me about something like it before I saw them, only I couldn’t recollect exactly what she told me. Then I wondered whether she had been the white lady, as I remembered she was just as white and beautiful, and had the same dark eyes and black hair; and sometimes she smiled and looked like the lady had looked, when she was telling me some of her stories, beginning with “Once on a time,” or “In the time of the fairies.” But I thought she couldn’t be the lady, as she seemed to have gone a different way into the wood, and I didn’t think the man who came after us could be the other, or I couldn’t have seen that wonderful secret in the secret wood. I thought of the moon: but it was afterwards when I was in the middle of the wild land, where the earth was made into the shape of great figures, and it was all walls, and mysterious hollows, and smooth round mounds, that I saw the great white moon come up over a round hill. I was wondering about all these things, till at last I got quite frightened, because I was afraid something had happened to me, and I remembered nurse’s tale of the poor girl who went into the hollow pit, and was carried away at last by the black man. I knew I had gone into a hollow pit too, and perhaps it was the same, and I had done something dreadful.

===Chinese===

虽然我房里黑黢黢、影影绰绰，一片淡淡的光却透过白窗帘照进来；有一回我起来朝外看，房子一大团黑影罩住了园子，像一座绞死人的监狱；影子外头一片白；林子白亮白亮的，树与树之间是一道道黑渊。天又静又清，天上没有一朵云。我想想我看见的东西，可想不起来，就开始想保姆很久以前讲过的那些故事——我以为早忘了，可它们全回来了，同灌木丛、灰石头、地上的圆坑、秘密的林子搅在一处，我几乎分不清哪些是新的、哪些是旧的，分不清这一切是不是做梦。随后我想起很久以前那个炎热的夏天下午，保姆把我一个人撂在荫凉里，白色人从水里、从林子里出来，玩耍、跳舞、唱歌；我开始觉得，早在看见他们之前，保姆就同我讲过有点像这个的事，只是她到底讲了什么，我记不真切了。随后我疑心，她就是那位白太太——我记得她也是那么白、那么美，也是那样的黑眼睛黑头发；有时她讲着故事微笑起来，就像那位太太笑过的样子——那些故事开头总是“从前呀”，或者“在仙人的年月里”。可我又想，她不会是那位太太，因为她像是走了另一条路进林子的；我也不信后来跟上来的那个男人是另一位，不然，我是不可能在那座秘密林子里看见那个奇妙的秘密的。我想起月亮：可那是在后来，我站在荒野当中、大地被做成人形的地方——到处是墙、神秘的圆坑、光溜溜的圆丘——我看见又大又白的月亮从圆山后头升上来。我这样那样地想着，末了自己害怕起来，怕自己出了什么事；我想起保姆讲的那个进了空坑、末了被黑男人带走的穷姑娘。我知道我也进过一个空坑，说不定是同一个，我也做了什么可怕的事。

===Original===

So I did the charm over again, and touched my eyes and my lips and my hair in a peculiar manner, and said the old words from the fairy language, so that I might be sure I had not been carried away. I tried again to see the secret wood, and to creep up the passage and see what I had seen there, but somehow I couldn’t, and I kept on thinking of nurse’s stories. There was one I remembered about a young man who once upon a time went hunting, and all the day he and his hounds hunted everywhere, and they crossed the rivers and went into all the woods, and went round the marshes, but they couldn’t find anything at all, and they hunted all day till the sun sank down and began to set behind the mountain. And the young man was angry because he couldn’t find anything, and he was going to turn back, when just as the sun touched the mountain, he saw come out of a brake in front of him a beautiful white stag. And he cheered to his hounds, but they whined and would not follow, and he cheered to his horse, but it shivered and stood stock still, and the young man jumped off the horse and left the hounds and began to follow the white stag all alone. And soon it was quite dark, and the sky was black, without a single star shining in it, and the stag went away into the darkness. And though the man had brought his gun with him he never shot at the stag, because he wanted to catch it, and he was afraid he would lose it in the night. But he never lost it once, though the sky was so black and the air was so dark, and the stag went on and on till the young man didn’t know a bit where he was. And they went through enormous woods where the air was full of whispers and a pale, dead light came out from the rotten trunks that were lying on the ground, and just as the man thought he had lost the stag, he would see it all white and shining in front of him, and he would run fast to catch it, but the stag always ran faster, so he did not catch it. And they went through the enormous woods, and they swam across rivers, and they waded through black marshes where the ground bubbled, and the air was full of will-o’-the-wisps, and the stag fled away down into rocky narrow valleys, where the air was like the smell of a vault, and the man went after it. And they went over the great mountains and the man heard the wind come down from the sky, and the stag went on and the man went after.

===Chinese===

于是我又把那道灵符做了一遍，用特别的姿势碰了眼睛、嘴唇和头发，念了仙语里的老字，好确证自己没有被带走。我又试着去看那秘密的林子，试着顺通道爬回去，看我在那里看见的东西，可不知怎么，看不成了；保姆的故事一个劲儿往心里来。有一个我记得，是讲从前有个年轻人去打猎，他带着猎犬找了一整天，趟过河，钻遍林子，绕过沼泽，什么也没找到；从早猎到太阳落山、挨着山头。年轻人什么也没猎着，正在气头上，打算回转，就在太阳碰着山的那一刻，他看见面前一丛灌木里出来一头美丽的白鹿。他吆喝猎犬，猎犬呜呜叫着不肯跟；他吆喝马，马打颤，钉在原地一动不动；年轻人跳下马，撇下猎犬，独自一个去追那头白鹿。不久天全黑了，天黑得一颗星也没有，鹿没进了黑暗里。那人带着枪，却始终没朝鹿开枪，因为他想活捉它，怕夜里失了它。可他一次也没失过它，虽然天那么黑、空气那么暗；鹿走啊走，走到年轻人完全不知道自己在哪儿。他们穿过一座座大得不得了的林子，林子里的空气满是低语，地上烂掉的树干里透出惨白的死光；每当那人以为失了鹿，就又看见它一身雪白、亮亮地在前头，他飞跑着去追，鹿总是跑得更快，所以总追不上。他们穿出大林子，游过一条条河，蹚过一片片黑沼泽，沼泽的地咕嘟咕嘟冒泡，空气里满是鬼火；鹿又逃下一条条多石的窄谷，谷里的空气像墓窖的气味，那人跟了下去。他们翻过一座座大山，那人听见风从天上下来；鹿在前头走，那人在后头追。

===Original===

At last the sun rose and the young man found he was in a country that he had never seen before; it was a beautiful valley with a bright stream running through it, and a great, big round hill in the middle. And the stag went down the valley, towards the hill, and it seemed to be getting tired and went slower and slower, and though the man was tired, too, he began to run faster, and he was sure he would catch the stag at last. But just as they got to the bottom of the hill, and the man stretched out his hand to catch the stag, it vanished into the earth, and the man began to cry; he was so sorry that he had lost it after all his long hunting. But as he was crying he saw there was a door in the hill, just in front of him, and he went in, and it was quite dark, but he went on, as he thought he would find the white stag. And all of a sudden it got light, and there was the sky, and the sun shining, and birds singing in the trees, and there was a beautiful fountain. And by the fountain a lovely lady was sitting, who was the queen of the fairies, and she told the man that she had changed herself into a stag to bring him there because she loved him so much. Then she brought out a great gold cup, covered with jewels, from her fairy palace, and she offered him wine in the cup to drink. And he drank, and the more he drank the more he longed to drink, because the wine was enchanted. So he kissed the lovely lady, and she became his wife, and he stayed all that day and all that night in the hill where she lived, and when he woke he found he was lying on the ground, close to where he had seen the stag first, and his horse was there and his hounds were there waiting, and he looked up, and the sun sank behind the mountain. And he went home and lived a long time, but he would never kiss any other lady because he had kissed the queen of the fairies, and he would never drink common wine any more, because he had drunk enchanted wine.

===Chinese===

末了太阳升起来，年轻人发现自己到了一片从没见过的乡野；那是一条美丽的山谷，一道亮亮的溪水从中间流过，当中一座又大又圆的山。鹿下了坡，朝山走去，像是累了，越走越慢；那人虽然也累了，却越跑越快，拿定主意终于要逮住它。可就在他们到了山脚、那人伸手去抓的时候，鹿没进了地里，那人哭起来；追了那么久还是失了它，他伤心极了。正哭着，他看见山体上有一扇门，就在他面前；他走了进去，里面黑透了，他还是往前走，心想能找到那头白鹿。忽然一下子亮了，头顶是天，太阳照着，鸟在树上唱歌，还有一座美丽的喷泉。喷泉边坐着一位可爱的太太，是仙后；她对那人说，她是自己变成一头鹿、把他引到那里来的，因为她太爱他了。随后她从她的仙宫里取出一柄镶满珠宝的大金杯，用杯盛酒请他喝。他喝了，越喝越想喝，因为那酒是施过法的。于是他吻了那位可爱的太太，她做了他的妻子；他在她住的山里待了一天一夜，一觉醒来，发现自己躺在地上，正在头一回看见鹿的地方跟前，他的马在，他的猎犬也在等着；他抬头一看，太阳正落到山背后。他回了家，活了很久；可他再也不肯吻别的女人，因为他吻过仙后；他再也不肯喝寻常的酒，因为他喝过施过法的酒。

===Original===

And sometimes nurse told me tales that she had heard from her great-grandmother, who was very old, and lived in a cottage on the mountain all alone, and most of these tales were about a hill where people used to meet at night long ago, and they used to play all sorts of strange games and do queer things that nurse told me of, but I couldn’t understand, and now, she said, everybody but her great-grandmother had forgotten all about it, and nobody knew where the hill was, not even her great-grandmother. But she told me one very strange story about the hill, and I trembled when I remembered it. She said that people always went there in summer, when it was very hot, and they had to dance a good deal. It would be all dark at first, and there were trees there, which made it much darker, and people would come, one by one, from all directions, by a secret path which nobody else knew, and two persons would keep the gate, and everyone as they came up had to give a very curious sign, which nurse showed me as well as she could, but she said she couldn’t show me properly. And all kinds of people would come; there would be gentle folks and village folks, and some old people and boys and girls, and quite small children, who sat and watched. And it would all be dark as they came in, except in one corner where someone was burning something that smelt strong and sweet, and made them laugh, and there one would see a glaring of coals, and the smoke mounting up red. So they would all come in, and when the last had come there was no door any more, so that no one else could get in, even if they knew there was anything beyond. And once a gentleman who was a stranger and had ridden a long way, lost his path at night, and his horse took him into the very middle of the wild country, where everything was upside down, and there were dreadful marshes and great stones everywhere, and holes underfoot, and the trees looked like gibbet-posts, because they had great black arms that stretched out across the way. And this strange gentleman was very frightened, and his horse began to shiver all over, and at last it stopped and wouldn’t go any farther, and the gentleman got down and tried to lead the horse, but it wouldn’t move, and it was all covered with a sweat, like death.

===Chinese===

保姆有时给我讲她从曾祖母那里听来的故事；曾祖母年纪很大了，一个人住在山上一座小屋里。这些故事多半是讲一座山，很久以前人们夜里常在那山上聚会，玩种种怪游戏，做种种古怪事——保姆讲给我听，可我听不懂；她说，如今除了她曾祖母，人人都忘光了，谁也不知道那山在哪儿，连她曾祖母也不知道。可她给我讲过一个关于那山的非常奇怪的故事，我一想起来就发抖。她说，人们总是在夏天、天最热的时候去，到了那儿要跳很多很多的舞。起先四下全黑，那里又有树，就黑上加黑；人们一个接一个从四面八方来，走一条别人都不知道的秘密小路；两个人守着门口，每个人上来了都得做一个很古怪的手势——保姆尽力比给我看过，可她说没法比划得真切。来的人形形色色；有上等人和村里人，有老人、男孩女孩，还有顶小的孩子，坐在一边看。他们进来时四下都黑，只有一个角落亮，那里有人烧着一种气味又浓又甜的东西，闻了叫人发笑；那个角落能看见炭火通红，烟红腾腾地往上冒。人们就这样一个个进来；最后一个进来之后，门就没有了，谁也别想再进来，哪怕他知道里面另有天地。有一回，一位外乡的绅士骑马走了很远的路，夜里迷了道，他的马把他驮进了荒野的正中心，那里一切都颠倒着，到处是可怕的沼泽和大石头，脚底下尽是窟窿，树一棵棵像绞刑架的柱子，因为它们伸出一条条大黑胳膊，横在路当中。这位陌生的绅士吓坏了，他的马发起抖来，浑身直颤，末了站住，再也不肯往前走；绅士下了马想牵着它走，它也不动，浑身出透了汗，像死了一样。

===Original===

So the gentleman went on all alone, going farther and farther into the wild country, till at last he came to a dark place, where he heard shouting and singing and crying, like nothing he had ever heard before. It all sounded quite close to him, but he couldn’t get in, and so he began to call, and while he was calling, something came behind him, and in a minute his mouth and arms and legs were all bound up, and he fell into a swoon. And when he came to himself, he was lying by the roadside, just where he had first lost his way, under a blasted oak with a black trunk, and his horse was tied beside him. So he rode on to the town and told the people there what had happened, and some of them were amazed; but others knew. So when once everybody had come, there was no door at all for anybody else to pass in by. And when they were all inside, round in a ring, touching each other, someone began to sing in the darkness, and someone else would make a noise like thunder with a thing they had on purpose, and on still nights people would hear the thundering noise far, far away beyond the wild land, and some of them, who thought they knew what it was, used to make a sign on their breasts when they woke up in their beds at dead of night and heard that terrible deep noise, like thunder on the mountains. And the noise and the singing would go on and on for a long time, and the people who were in a ring swayed a little to and fro; and the song was in an old, old language that nobody knows now, and the tune was queer. Nurse said her great-grandmother had known someone who remembered a little of it, when she was quite a little girl, and nurse tried to sing some of it to me, and it was so strange a tune that I turned all cold and my flesh crept as if I had put my hand on something dead. Sometimes it was a man that sang and sometimes it was a woman, and sometimes the one who sang it did it so well that two or three of the people who were there fell to the ground shrieking and tearing with their hands. The singing went on, and the people in the ring kept swaying to and fro for a long time, and at last the moon would rise over a place they called the Tole Deol, and came up and showed them swinging and swaying from side to side, with the sweet thick smoke curling up from the burning coals, and floating in circles all around them. Then they had their supper.

===Chinese===

于是绅士独自往前走，越走越深，走进荒野，末了到了一个黑地方，听见里头有喊、有唱、有哭，同他这辈子听过的什么都不像。声音听着就在跟前，可他进不去，就喊起来；正喊着，有什么东西到了他背后，一转眼，他的嘴、胳膊、腿全被捆住了，他晕了过去。等他醒过来，自己躺在路边，正在他起初迷路的地方，在一棵焦黑的枯橡树底下，他的马拴在旁边。他骑马进了城，把遇到的事告诉城里人；有的惊呆了；有的心里明白。所以说，等人都到齐了，就再没有门给任何人进来了。等他们全进了里头，围成一圈，彼此挨着，黑暗里有人开始唱歌，另一个人用一件特备的家什弄出打雷似的响声；无风的夜里，荒野那头很远很远的地方都听得见那雷声；有些人自以为知道那是什么，夜半在床上惊醒、听见那可怕的低沉响声——像山上的雷——就在自己胸口画一个记号。响声和歌声要持续很久很久，围成圈的人轻轻前后摇晃；歌是一种极老极老的语言唱的，如今谁也不懂了，调子怪得很。保姆说，她曾祖母认得一个人，那人小时候还记得一点；保姆试着给我唱过几句，那调子怪得我浑身发冷，肉皮直往上爬，像把手放到了什么死东西上。有时是男人唱，有时是女人唱；有时唱的人唱得太好，在场的人有两三个会栽倒在地，尖叫着用手乱抓。唱着唱着，圈里的人一直摇啊晃啊，摇了很久；末了，月亮升上他们叫作托勒迪奥尔（Tole Deol）的地方，升上来，照见他们左摇右摆，甜浓的烟从烧红的炭上打着旋儿升起来，一圈一圈绕着他们飘。随后他们就吃晚饭。

===Original===

A boy and a girl brought it to them; the boy carried a great cup of wine, and the girl carried a cake of bread, and they passed the bread and the wine round and round, but they tasted quite different from common bread and common wine, and changed everybody that tasted them. Then they all rose up and danced, and secret things were brought out of some hiding place, and they played extraordinary games, and danced round and round and round in the moonlight, and sometimes people would suddenly disappear and never be heard of afterwards, and nobody knew what had happened to them. And they drank more of that curious wine, and they made images and worshipped them, and nurse showed me how the images were made one day when we were out for a walk, and we passed by a place where there was a lot of wet clay. So nurse asked me if I would like to know what those things were like that they made on the hill, and I said yes. Then she asked me if I would promise never to tell a living soul a word about it, and if I did I was to be thrown into the black pit with the dead people, and I said I wouldn’t tell anybody, and she said the same thing again and again, and I promised. So she took my wooden spade and dug a big lump of clay and put it in my tin bucket, and told me to say if anyone met us that I was going to make pies when I went home. Then we went on a little way till we came to a little brake growing right down into the road, and nurse stopped, and looked up the road and down it, and then peeped through the hedge into the field on the other side, and then she said, “Quick!” and we ran into the brake, and crept in and out among the bushes till we had gone a good way from the road. Then we sat down under a bush, and I wanted so much to know what nurse was going to make with the clay, but before she would begin she made me promise again not to say a word about it, and she went again and peeped through the bushes on every side, though the lane was so small and deep that hardly anybody ever went there. So we sat down, and nurse took the clay out of the bucket, and began to knead it with her hands, and do queer things with it, and turn it about.

===Chinese===

一个男孩和一个女孩给他们送饭来；男孩捧一大杯酒，女孩捧一大块饼；他们把饼和酒一圈圈传下去，可那味道同寻常的饼、寻常的酒全然不同，尝过的人个个都变了。随后大家起身跳舞，秘密的东西从什么藏处搬了出来，他们玩起种种怪游戏，在月光里一圈一圈转着跳；有时会有人忽然不见了，从此再没有音讯，谁也不知道出了什么事。他们又喝那种怪酒，又捏神像来拜。有一天我们出去散步，路过一块有好些湿泥的地方，保姆就把神像的捏法指给我看。她问我，想不想知道他们在山上做的那些东西是什么样子，我说想。她就问我肯不肯起誓，永远不向一个活人透露半个字；要是说了，就要同死人一起被扔进黑坑。我说我谁也不告诉；她把这话又说了一遍又一遍，我起了誓。她就拿起我的小木铲，挖了一大块泥，放进我的小铁桶，教我路上遇着人，就说我回家要捏泥饼。随后我们走了一小段，来到一处灌木丛一直长到路边的地方；保姆停下，朝路两头望了望，又隔着树篱朝那边的田里瞄了瞄，说了声“快！”我们就跑进灌木丛，在树丛里钻进钻出，直到离路很远了才坐下，坐在一丛灌木底下。我特别特别想知道保姆要用泥捏什么，可她动手之前又叫我起了一回誓，又到四面八方的灌木缝里张望了一遍——虽然那条小路又窄又深，几乎从没有人走。于是我们坐下来，保姆把泥从桶里拿出来，用双手揉它，对它做种种怪事，把它翻过来掉过去。

===Original===

And she hid it under a big dock-leaf for a minute or two and then she brought it out again, and then she stood up and sat down, and walked round the clay in a peculiar manner, and all the time she was softly singing a sort of rhyme, and her face got very red. Then she sat down again, and took the clay in her hands and began to shape it into a doll, but not like the dolls I have at home, and she made the queerest doll I had ever seen, all out of the wet clay, and hid it under a bush to get dry and hard, and all the time she was making it she was singing these rhymes to herself, and her face got redder and redder. So we left the doll there, hidden away in the bushes where nobody would ever find it. And a few days later we went the same walk, and when we came to that narrow, dark part of the lane where the brake runs down to the bank, nurse made me promise all over again, and she looked about, just as she had done before, and we crept into the bushes till we got to the green place where the little clay man was hidden. I remember it all so well, though I was only eight, and it is eight years ago now as I am writing it down, but the sky was a deep violet blue, and in the middle of the brake where we were sitting there was a great elder tree covered with blossoms, and on the other side there was a clump of meadowsweet, and when I think of that day the smell of the meadowsweet and elder blossom seems to fill the room, and if I shut my eyes I can see the glaring blue sky, with little clouds very white floating across it, and nurse who went away long ago sitting opposite me and looking like the beautiful white lady in the wood. So we sat down and nurse took out the clay doll from the secret place where she had hidden it, and she said we must “pay our respects,” and she would show me what to do, and I must watch her all the time. So she did all sorts of queer things with the little clay man, and I noticed she was all streaming with perspiration, though we had walked so slowly, and then she told me to “pay my respects,” and I did everything she did because I liked her, and it was such an odd game. And she said that if one loved very much, the clay man was very good, if one did certain things with it, and if one hated very much, it was just as good, only one had to do different things, and we played with it a long time, and pretended all sorts of things.

===Chinese===

她把泥在一片大酸模叶子底下藏了一两分钟，又拿出来；随后她站起来又坐下，用一种特别的步子绕着那块泥走；一路上她低低唱着一种歌谣，脸涨得通红。随后她又坐下，把泥拿在手里，开始捏成一个娃娃，可同我家里的那些娃娃都不像；她捏出了我见过的最古怪的娃娃，全用湿泥捏的，然后把它藏到一丛灌木底下晾干晾硬。捏的时候她一直给自己哼着那些歌谣，脸越涨越红。我们就把那娃娃留在那里了，藏在灌木丛里谁也找不到的地方。几天之后我们又走了那条路，走到小路又窄又黑、灌木丛一直长到沟沿的那一段，保姆又叫我从头起了一回誓，又同上回一样四下张望，我们钻进灌木丛，一直爬到藏小泥人的那块绿地方。这一切我记得清清楚楚，虽然那时我只有八岁，如今写下这些，已经是八年前的事了；那天的天是深深的紫蓝色，我们坐的那片灌木丛正中有一大棵接骨木，开满了花；另一边是一丛旋果蚊子草。一想起那天，旋果蚊子草和接骨木花的香气仿佛就灌满屋子；一闭上眼，我就看得见那亮得扎眼的蓝天，一小朵一小朵雪白的云飘过去；还看得见早就走了的保姆，坐在我对面，像林子里那位美丽的白太太。于是我们坐下，保姆从藏的地方取出那个泥娃娃，说我们必须“行礼”，她做给我看怎么做，我得时刻盯着她。于是她对那小泥人做了种种怪事；我注意到她浑身淌汗，虽然我们走得那么慢。随后她叫我“行礼”；她做什么我就做什么，因为我喜欢她，再说那游戏实在好玩。她说，人要是爱得非常深，只要对泥人做某些事，泥人就非常灵；要是恨得非常深，它也一样灵，只是要做的事不同。我们玩了它很久很久，假装了种种事情。

===Original===

Nurse said her great-grandmother had told her all about these images, but what we did was no harm at all, only a game. But she told me a story about these images that frightened me very much, and that was what I remembered that night when I was lying awake in my room in the pale, empty darkness, thinking of what I had seen and the secret wood. Nurse said there was once a young lady of the high gentry, who lived in a great castle. And she was so beautiful that all the gentlemen wanted to marry her, because she was the loveliest lady that anybody had ever seen, and she was kind to everybody, and everybody thought she was very good. But though she was polite to all the gentlemen who wished to marry her, she put them off, and said she couldn’t make up her mind, and she wasn’t sure she wanted to marry anybody at all. And her father, who was a very great lord, was angry, though he was so fond of her, and he asked her why she wouldn’t choose a bachelor out of all the handsome young men who came to the castle. But she only said she didn’t love any of them very much, and she must wait, and if they pestered her, she said she would go and be a nun in a nunnery. So all the gentlemen said they would go away and wait for a year and a day, and when a year and a day were gone, they would come back again and ask her to say which one she would marry. So the day was appointed and they all went away; and the lady had promised that in a year and a day it would be her wedding day with one of them. But the truth was, that she was the queen of the people who danced on the hill on summer nights, and on the proper nights she would lock the door of her room, and she and her maid would steal out of the castle by a secret passage that only they knew of, and go away up to the hill in the wild land. And she knew more of the secret things than anyone else, and more than anyone knew before or after, because she would not tell anybody the most secret secrets. She knew how to do all the awful things, how to destroy young men, and how to put a curse on people, and other things that I could not understand. And her real name was the Lady Avelin, but the dancing people called her Cassap, which meant somebody very wise, in the old language.

===Chinese===

保姆说，这些神像的事，她曾祖母全告诉过她；不过我们做的一点害处也没有，只是个游戏。可她给我讲了一个关于神像的故事，把我吓坏了；那天夜里我醒着躺在自己房里、躺在惨白空洞的黑暗里想着我看见的东西和那秘密林子时，想起的就是它。保姆说，从前有一位高贵门第的年轻小姐，住在一座大城堡里。她美极了，绅士们个个都想娶她，因为她是人人见过的最可爱的小姐；她对谁都和气，人人也都当她是个顶好的人。可对那些想娶她的绅士们，她虽然客客气气，却总把他们往后拖，说拿不定主意，说不见得想嫁人。她父亲是个很大的爵爷，虽然疼她，也生了气，问她为什么不来堡里来的那些漂亮年轻人里挑一个。她只说她不大爱他们当中的任何一个，她得等等；他们要是逼她，她就进修道院做修女去。于是绅士们都说，他们走开，等一年零一天；一年零一天过去，他们再回来，请她说出嫁哪一个。日子就这样约定了，他们都走了；小姐也应承了：一年零一天之后，就是她同他们中一个的婚期。可实情是：她就是夏夜里在山上跳舞的那些人的王后。到了该去的夜里，她就锁上自己房门，同她的贴身丫头一起，从一条只有她们知道的秘道溜出城堡，往荒野的山上去。秘密的事，她比谁都懂得多，比她前后任何人懂得的都多，因为顶秘密的秘密她谁也不告诉。那些可怕的事她全会：怎样毁掉年轻人，怎样下诅咒，还有别的我听不懂的事。她真名叫阿弗琳小姐（Lady Avelin），可跳舞的人们叫她卡萨普（Cassap），在老语言里，那意思是顶有智慧的人。

===Original===

And she was whiter than any of them and taller, and her eyes shone in the dark like burning rubies; and she could sing songs that none of the others could sing, and when she sang they all fell down on their faces and worshipped her. And she could do what they called shib-show, which was a very wonderful enchantment. She would tell the great lord, her father, that she wanted to go into the woods to gather flowers, so he let her go, and she and her maid went into the woods where nobody came, and the maid would keep watch. Then the lady would lie down under the trees and begin to sing a particular song, and she stretched out her arms, and from every part of the wood great serpents would come, hissing and gliding in and out among the trees, and shooting out their forked tongues as they crawled up to the lady. And they all came to her, and twisted round her, round her body, and her arms, and her neck, till she was covered with writhing serpents, and there was only her head to be seen. And she whispered to them, and she sang to them, and they writhed round and round, faster and faster, till she told them to go. And they all went away directly, back to their holes, and on the lady’s breast there would be a most curious, beautiful stone, shaped something like an egg, and coloured dark blue and yellow, and red, and green, marked like a serpent’s scales. It was called a glame stone, and with it one could do all sorts of wonderful things, and nurse said her great-grandmother had seen a glame stone with her own eyes, and it was for all the world shiny and scaly like a snake. And the lady could do a lot of other things as well, but she was quite fixed that she would not be married. And there were a great many gentlemen who wanted to marry her, but there were five of them who were chief, and their names were Sir Simon, Sir John, Sir Oliver, Sir Richard, and Sir Rowland. All the others believed she spoke the truth, and that she would choose one of them to be her man when a year and a day was done; it was only Sir Simon, who was very crafty, who thought she was deceiving them all, and he vowed he would watch and try if he could find out anything.

===Chinese===

她比他们全都白、比他们全都高，眼睛在黑暗里像烧着的红宝石一样发亮；她会唱别人谁也不会唱的歌，她一唱，众人就都伏下脸去拜她。她还会做他们叫作希布肖（shib-show）的东西，那是一种非常奇妙的法术。她会禀告父亲大人说想进林子采花，他就放她去；她同丫头进了没人来的林子，丫头望风。小姐就在树下躺下来，开始唱一支特别的歌，张开两条胳膊；林子的每个角落就会游出一条条大蛇，嘶嘶叫着在树间穿进穿出，吐着叉子舌，朝小姐爬过去。它们全都来到她跟前，缠上她，缠她的身子、她的胳膊、她的脖子，直到她浑身缠满了扭动的大蛇，只露出一颗头。她对它们低语，对它们唱歌，它们一圈一圈越缠越快、越缠越快，直到她叫它们走。它们立刻全散了，各回各的洞；小姐的胸口就多了一块顶古怪、顶美的石头，形状有点像蛋，颜色是深蓝、黄、红、绿，花纹像蛇鳞。那叫格拉姆石（glame stone），有了它，什么奇妙的事都能做。保姆说，她曾祖母亲眼见过一块格拉姆石，说它活脱脱就是蛇那样，又亮又鳞。小姐还会做许多别的事，可她打定了主意不嫁人。想娶她的绅士很多，为首的有五位：西蒙爵士、约翰爵士、奥利弗爵士、理查爵士和罗兰爵士。别人全都信她的话，信一年零一天到期时她会从他们中间挑一个做丈夫；只有西蒙爵士，他顶顶精明，疑心她把大家全骗了，起誓要暗中察看，看能不能查出点什么。

===Original===

And though he was very wise he was very young, and he had a smooth, soft face like a girl’s, and he pretended, as the rest did, that he would not come to the castle for a year and a day, and he said he was going away beyond the sea to foreign parts. But he really only went a very little way, and came back dressed like a servant girl, and so he got a place in the castle to wash the dishes. And he waited and watched, and he listened and said nothing, and he hid in dark places, and woke up at night and looked out, and he heard things and he saw things that he thought were very strange. And he was so sly that he told the girl that waited on the lady that he was really a young man, and that he had dressed up as a girl because he loved her so very much and wanted to be in the same house with her, and the girl was so pleased that she told him many things, and he was more than ever certain that the Lady Avelin was deceiving him and the others. And he was so clever, and told the servant so many lies, that one night he managed to hide in the Lady Avelin’s room behind the curtains. And he stayed quite still and never moved, and at last the lady came. And she bent down under the bed, and raised up a stone, and there was a hollow place underneath, and out of it she took a waxen image, just like the clay one that I and nurse had made in the brake. And all the time her eyes were burning like rubies. And she took the little wax doll up in her arms and held it to her breast, and she whispered and she murmured, and she took it up and she laid it down again, and she held it high, and she held it low, and she laid it down again. And she said, “Happy is he that begat the bishop, that ordered the clerk, that married the man, that had the wife, that fashioned the hive, that harboured the bee, that gathered the wax that my own true love was made of.” And she brought out of an aumbry a great golden bowl, and she brought out of a closet a great jar of wine, and she poured some of the wine into the bowl, and she laid her mannikin very gently in the wine, and washed it in the wine all over. Then she went to a cupboard and took a small round cake and laid it on the image’s mouth, and then she bore it softly and covered it up.

===Chinese===

他虽然极聪明，人却很年轻，一张脸又光又软，像女孩儿。他也照旁人的样子，假意说一年零一天之内不进城堡，还说他要漂洋过海去外国。其实他只走了不多远就回来了，装扮成一个使女，进城堡找了个洗碟子的差事。他等着，看着，听着，什么也不说；他藏在暗处，半夜醒来张望；他听见了些事，也看见了些事，觉得都非常古怪。他花招极多，竟对伺候小姐的那个丫头说，自己其实是个年轻男人，扮成姑娘是因为太爱她，想同她住在一座房子里；丫头欢喜得不得了，告诉了他许多事；他于是比先前更拿得准：阿弗琳小姐骗了他和众人。他机灵得很，又对那丫头撒了许多谎，一天夜里，居然藏进了阿弗琳小姐的房间里，躲在帐幔后面。他一动也不动；末了小姐回来了。她朝床底下弯下腰，掀起一块石头，底下是一个洞，从里面取出一个蜡像，同我和保姆在灌木丛里捏的那个泥人一模一样。她那双眼睛一直像红宝石一样烧着。她把小蜡人抱在怀里，贴在胸口，低语着，呢喃着；她把它举起来，又放下；举得高高的，又低低的，又放下。她念道：“生主教的有福了——主教吩咐下教士，教士成全了婚事，婚事成全了妻子，妻子筑了蜂巢，蜂巢收留了蜜蜂，蜜蜂采回了蜡——我那心上人，就是这蜡做成的。”随后她从圣柜里捧出一只大金碗，从橱里抱出一大罐酒，往碗里倒了些酒，把她的蜡人儿轻轻放进酒里，用酒把它周身洗了一遍。随后她走到碗柜前，取出一块小圆饼，放在蜡像嘴上，然后把它轻轻捧起，盖上收好。

===Original===

And Sir Simon, who was watching all the time, though he was terribly frightened, saw the lady bend down and stretch out her arms and whisper and sing, and then Sir Simon saw beside her a handsome young man, who kissed her on the lips. And they drank wine out of the golden bowl together, and they ate the cake together. But when the sun rose there was only the little wax doll, and the lady hid it again under the bed in the hollow place. So Sir Simon knew quite well what the lady was, and he waited and he watched, till the time she had said was nearly over, and in a week the year and a day would be done. And one night, when he was watching behind the curtains in her room, he saw her making more wax dolls. And she made five, and hid them away. And the next night she took one out, and held it up, and filled the golden bowl with water, and took the doll by the neck and held it under the water. Then she said﻿—

Sir Dickon, Sir Dickon, your day is done,

You shall be drowned in the water wan.

And the next day news came to the castle that Sir Richard had been drowned at the ford. And at night she took another doll and tied a violet cord round its neck and hung it up on a nail. Then she said﻿—

Sir Rowland, your life has ended its span,

High on a tree I see you hang.

And the next day news came to the castle that Sir Rowland had been hanged by robbers in the wood. And at night she took another doll, and drove her bodkin right into its heart. Then she said﻿—

Sir Noll, Sir Noll, so cease your life,

Your heart piercèd with the knife.

And the next day news came to the castle that Sir Oliver had fought in a tavern, and a stranger had stabbed him to the heart. And at night she took another doll, and held it to a fire of charcoal till it was melted. Then she said﻿—

Sir John, return, and turn to clay,

In fire of fever you waste away.

===Chinese===

西蒙爵士一直在偷看，吓得魂都没有了；他看见小姐俯下身，张开双臂，低语，歌唱；随后西蒙爵士看见她身边站着一个英俊的年轻人，吻她的嘴唇。他们同用金碗喝了酒，同分吃了那块饼。可太阳升起来时，那里只剩那个小蜡人，小姐又把它藏回床下的洞里。西蒙爵士这下全明白了小姐是什么；他等着，看着，直到她约定的日子快完，再过一星期，一年零一天就到期了。一天夜里，他在她房里帐幔后看着，见她在捏更多蜡人。她捏了五个，藏了起来。第二天夜里，她取出一个，举起来，往金碗里倒满了水，捏住蜡人的脖子，把它按进水里。随后念道——

狄肯爵士，狄肯爵士，你的日子完了，
你将淹死在惨白的水里。

第二天，消息传到城堡：理查爵士在渡口淹死了。夜里她又取出一个蜡人，用一根紫罗兰色的绳拴住脖子，挂在钉子上。随后念道——

罗兰爵士，你的命数已尽，
高高的树上，我见你悬身。

第二天，消息传到城堡：罗兰爵士在林中被强盗吊死了。夜里她又取出一个，把长针直直扎进它的心口。随后念道——

诺尔爵士，诺尔爵士，你的命到此为止，
你的心口被刀刺穿。

第二天，消息传到城堡：奥利弗爵士在酒馆里同人斗殴，被一个生人一刀刺穿了心口。夜里她又取出一个蜡人，举在炭火上烤，直到烤化了。随后念道——

约翰爵士，归来吧，归于泥土，
热病之火把你烧枯。

===Original===

And the next day news came to the castle that Sir John had died in a burning fever. So then Sir Simon went out of the castle and mounted his horse and rode away to the bishop and told him everything. And the bishop sent his men, and they took the Lady Avelin, and everything she had done was found out. So on the day after the year and a day, when she was to have been married, they carried her through the town in her smock, and they tied her to a great stake in the marketplace, and burned her alive before the bishop with her wax image hung round her neck. And people said the wax man screamed in the burning of the flames. And I thought of this story again and again as I was lying awake in my bed, and I seemed to see the Lady Avelin in the marketplace, with the yellow flames eating up her beautiful white body. And I thought of it so much that I seemed to get into the story myself, and I fancied I was the lady, and that they were coming to take me to be burnt with fire, with all the people in the town looking at me. And I wondered whether she cared, after all the strange things she had done, and whether it hurt very much to be burned at the stake. I tried again and again to forget nurse’s stories, and to remember the secret I had seen that afternoon, and what was in the secret wood, but I could only see the dark and a glimmering in the dark, and then it went away, and I only saw myself running, and then a great moon came up white over a dark round hill. Then all the old stories came back again, and the queer rhymes that nurse used to sing to me; and there was one beginning “Halsy cumsy Helen musty,” that she used to sing very softly when she wanted me to go to sleep. And I began to sing it to myself inside of my head, and I went to sleep.

===Chinese===

第二天，消息传到城堡：约翰爵士发着高烧死了。于是西蒙爵士出了城堡，上了马，跑去见主教，把一切都告诉了他。主教派了人，把阿弗琳小姐捉了来，她做过的事全查了出来。于是一年零一天的第二天——本该是她成婚的日子——他们让她只穿一身衬衣，押着游过全城，把她绑在集市广场的大桩子上，当着主教的面活活烧死，脖子上还挂着她的蜡像。人们说，烈火里那个蜡人发出了尖叫。我醒着躺在床上，把这个故事想了一遍又一遍；我仿佛看见阿弗琳小姐站在集市上，黄色的火苗正吞吃她美丽的白身体。我想得那样多，仿佛自己也走进了故事里；我把自己当成了那位小姐，他们正来捉我去受火刑，全城的人都看着我。我纳闷，做了那么多怪事之后，她到底在乎不在乎；绑在桩子上烧，到底疼不疼，疼得厉害不厉害。我一遍又一遍想忘掉保姆的故事，去想那天下午看见的秘密、秘密林子里的东西；可我只看得见黑暗，和黑暗里一点微光；随后它也去了，只剩我自己在奔跑，随后一轮又大又白的月亮从黑黑的圆山后头升起来。随后，那些老故事又全回来了，还有保姆唱给我听的那些怪歌谣；有一支开头是“哈尔西·卡姆西·海伦·马斯蒂”的，她哄我睡觉时总是轻轻唱。我便在心里给自己唱起来，唱着唱着就睡着了。

===Original===

The next morning I was very tired and sleepy, and could hardly do my lessons, and I was very glad when they were over and I had had my dinner, as I wanted to go out and be alone. It was a warm day, and I went to a nice turfy hill by the river, and sat down on my mother’s old shawl that I had brought with me on purpose. The sky was grey, like the day before, but there was a kind of white gleam behind it, and from where I was sitting I could look down on the town, and it was all still and quiet and white, like a picture. I remembered that it was on that hill that nurse taught me to play an old game called “Troy Town,” in which one had to dance, and wind in and out on a pattern in the grass, and then when one had danced and turned long enough the other person asks you questions, and you can’t help answering whether you want to or not, and whatever you are told to do you feel you have to do it. Nurse said there used to be a lot of games like that that some people knew of, and there was one by which people could be turned into anything you liked, and an old man her great-grandmother had seen had known a girl who had been turned into a large snake. And there was another very ancient game of dancing and winding and turning, by which you could take a person out of himself and hide him away as long as you liked, and his body went walking about quite empty, without any sense in it. But I came to that hill because I wanted to think of what had happened the day before, and of the secret of the wood. From the place where I was sitting I could see beyond the town, into the opening I had found, where a little brook had led me into an unknown country. And I pretended I was following the brook over again, and I went all the way in my mind, and at last I found the wood, and crept into it under the bushes, and then in the dusk I saw something that made me feel as if I were filled with fire, as if I wanted to dance and sing and fly up into the air, because I was changed and wonderful. But what I saw was not changed at all, and had not grown old, and I wondered again and again how such things could happen, and whether nurse’s stories were really true, because in the daytime in the open air everything seemed quite different from what it was at night, when I was frightened, and thought I was to be burned alive.

===Chinese===

第二天早晨我又累又困，功课几乎做不动；好容易功课完了、饭也吃了，我欢喜得很，因为我要出去独自待着。那天很暖和，我走到河边一座长满草皮的可爱小山上，坐在特意带来的妈妈那条旧披巾上。天是灰的，同前一天一样，可灰后头有一种白光；从我坐的地方可以俯望那座城，它安安静静、白白净净，像一幅画。我记得，保姆就是在那座山上教我玩一种叫“特洛伊城”的老游戏的：人要跳着舞，沿着草地上的花纹穿进穿出；跳得转得够久了，另一个人就问你话，你不管愿不愿意，都忍不住要答，叫你做什么，你就觉得非做不可。保姆说，从前有许多这样的游戏，有些人懂；有一种能把人变成随你喜欢的任何东西，她曾祖母见过的一个老头儿，认得一个变成过大蛇的姑娘。还有一种顶老顶老的跳舞转圈的游戏，能把一个人从他身体里引出来，随你藏多久，他的身子就空空地走来走去，里面一点知觉也没有。可我到那山上去，是因为要想想前一天发生的事，想想林子的秘密。从我坐的地方望过去，越过城，能望见我发现的那个入口，那条小溪就是从那里把我引进未知乡野的。我假装又在顺着小溪走，在心里把全程走了一遍；末了找到了那片林子，从灌木底下爬进去；随后在暮色里看见了那个东西，它叫我浑身像灌满了火，想跳舞，想唱歌，想飞上天去，因为我变了，变得奇妙了。可我看见的那样东西一点也没变，也没有变老；我一遍又一遍地纳闷，这样的事怎么可能发生，保姆的故事到底是不是真的——因为白天、在露天底下，样样东西都同夜里大不一样；夜里我害怕，想着自己要被活活烧死了。

===Original===

I once told my father one of her little tales, which was about a ghost, and asked him if it was true, and he told me it was not true at all, and that only common, ignorant people believed in such rubbish. He was very angry with nurse for telling me the story, and scolded her, and after that I promised her I would never whisper a word of what she told me, and if I did I should be bitten by the great black snake that lived in the pool in the wood. And all alone on the hill I wondered what was true. I had seen something very amazing and very lovely, and I knew a story, and if I had really seen it, and not made it up out of the dark, and the black bough, and the bright shining that was mounting up to the sky from over the great round hill, but had really seen it in truth, then there were all kinds of wonderful and lovely and terrible things to think of, so I longed and trembled, and I burned and got cold. And I looked down on the town, so quiet and still, like a little white picture, and I thought over and over if it could be true. I was a long time before I could make up my mind to anything; there was such a strange fluttering at my heart that seemed to whisper to me all the time that I had not made it up out of my head, and yet it seemed quite impossible, and I knew my father and everybody would say it was dreadful rubbish. I never dreamed of telling him or anybody else a word about it, because I knew it would be of no use, and I should only get laughed at or scolded, so for a long time I was very quiet, and went about thinking and wondering; and at night I used to dream of amazing things, and sometimes I woke up in the early morning and held out my arms with a cry. And I was frightened, too, because there were dangers, and some awful thing would happen to me, unless I took great care, if the story were true. These old tales were always in my head, night and morning, and I went over them and told them to myself over and over again, and went for walks in the places where nurse had told them to me; and when I sat in the nursery by the fire in the evenings I used to fancy nurse was sitting in the other chair, and telling me some wonderful story in a low voice, for fear anybody should be listening.

===Chinese===

有一回我把她的一个小故事——讲鬼的——讲给父亲听，问他是不是真的；他说根本不是真的，只有下等的、无知的人才会信这种垃圾。他为保姆讲这故事大大生了一场气，骂了她。打那以后，我向她起誓，她告诉我的事，我一个字也绝不漏出去；要是漏了，就叫林子水潭里住着的那条大黑蛇咬我。我独自坐在山上，纳闷到底什么是真的。我看见过一样顶惊人、顶可爱的东西，我还知道一个故事；倘若我不是从黑暗里、从黑树枝里、从大圆山后头升上天的亮光里把它编出来的，而是真真切切看见了它，那就有一切奇妙、可爱又可怕的事情好想了；于是我又是渴望，又是发抖，一阵阵发热，一阵阵发冷。我俯望那座城，那么安静，那么纹丝不动，像一幅小小的白画；我翻来覆去地想：这会不会是真的。我好久好久拿不定主意；我心里有一种奇怪的扑腾，一直低声对我说，这不是我从脑袋里编出来的；可这事看着又全然不可能，我知道父亲和所有人都会说这是可怕的垃圾。我做梦也没想过告诉他或任何人一个字，因为我知道没有用，只会招来嘲笑或训斥；于是好长时间我都很安静，走来走去地想、纳闷；夜里我净做些惊人的梦，有时清早醒来，喊一声，张开两条胳膊。我也害怕，因为有危险；倘若那故事是真的，我一不小心，就会有可怕的事落到我头上。这些老故事早晚都在我脑子里，我一遍遍温习，一遍遍讲给自己听，还到保姆当年讲它们的地方去散步；傍晚我坐在育婴室炉火边，常常恍惚觉得保姆就坐在对面那把椅子里，正压低了嗓子给我讲什么好听的故事，生怕有人偷听。

===Original===

But she used to like best to tell me about things when we were right out in the country, far from the house, because she said she was telling me such secrets, and walls have ears. And if it was something more than ever secret, we had to hide in brakes or woods; and I used to think it was such fun creeping along a hedge, and going very softly, and then we would get behind the bushes or run into the wood all of a sudden, when we were sure that none was watching us; so we knew that we had our secrets quite all to ourselves, and nobody else at all knew anything about them. Now and then, when we had hidden ourselves as I have described, she used to show me all sorts of odd things. One day, I remember, we were in a hazel brake, overlooking the brook, and we were so snug and warm, as though it was April; the sun was quite hot, and the leaves were just coming out. Nurse said she would show me something funny that would make me laugh, and then she showed me, as she said, how one could turn a whole house upside down, without anybody being able to find out, and the pots and pans would jump about, and the china would be broken, and the chairs would tumble over of themselves. I tried it one day in the kitchen, and I found I could do it quite well, and a whole row of plates on the dresser fell off it, and cook’s little worktable tilted up and turned right over “before her eyes,” as she said, but she was so frightened and turned so white that I didn’t do it again, as I liked her. And afterwards, in the hazel copse, when she had shown me how to make things tumble about, she showed me how to make rapping noises, and I learnt how to do that, too. Then she taught me rhymes to say on certain occasions, and peculiar marks to make on other occasions, and other things that her great-grandmother had taught her when she was a little girl herself. And these were all the things I was thinking about in those days after the strange walk when I thought I had seen a great secret, and I wished nurse were there for me to ask her about it, but she had gone away more than two years before, and nobody seemed to know what had become of her, or where she had gone.

===Chinese===

可她最喜欢在乡下、离屋子远远的地方给我讲这些，因为她说，她讲的是这样的秘密，而墙有耳朵。若是顶顶秘密的事，我们就得躲进灌木丛或林子里；我总觉得，贴着树篱蹑手蹑脚地走可太好玩了；等确信没有人看我们，就一下子钻到灌木后头，或跑进林子里去。于是我们知道，这些秘密全归我们自己，别的人一丝一毫也不知道。偶尔，等我们照我说的那样藏好了，她就给我看种种怪东西。记得有一天，我们在一处榛子丛里，俯瞰着小溪，又暖和又惬意，像四月里的天气；太阳挺晒，叶子刚冒出来。保姆说要给我看个逗笑的东西，随后她教我——按她的说法——怎样把一座整屋子闹个底朝天，还叫谁也查不出来：锅碗瓢盆乱蹦，瓷器摔碎，椅子自己翻倒。有一天我在厨房里试了试，发现我干得挺不错：碗柜上整整一排盘子掉了下去，厨娘那张小工作台翘起来，当着“她自己的眼睛”整个翻了过去——她自己是这样说的；可她吓得脸都白了，我后来没有再做，因为我喜欢她。后来在榛子丛里，她教完我怎样让东西乱蹦乱跳，又教我怎样弄出敲击声，这个我也学会了。随后她教我在某些场合念的歌谣、在另一些场合画的特别记号，还有别的许多东西，都是她小时候她曾祖母教她的。这些就是那些天我心里想着的一切——自从那场怪异的散步、自从我想我看见了一个大秘密之后；我真希望保姆还在，好去问她；可她两年多前就走了，好像谁也不知道她后来怎样了，去了哪里。

===Original===

But I shall always remember those days if I live to be quite old, because all the time I felt so strange, wondering and doubting, and feeling quite sure at one time, and making up my mind, and then I would feel quite sure that such things couldn’t happen really, and it began all over again. But I took great care not to do certain things that might be very dangerous. So I waited and wondered for a long time, and though I was not sure at all, I never dared to try to find out. But one day I became sure that all that nurse said was quite true, and I was all alone when I found it out. I trembled all over with joy and terror, and as fast as I could I ran into one of the old brakes where we used to go﻿—it was the one by the lane, where nurse made the little clay man﻿—and I ran into it, and I crept into it; and when I came to the place where the elder was, I covered up my face with my hands and lay down flat on the grass, and I stayed there for two hours without moving, whispering to myself delicious, terrible things, and saying some words over and over again.
